We then prove that the two concentration points are identical. For simplicity, we only discuss the case when
\nu_{2}>\ldots>\nu_{p}
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{2}>\ldots>\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}}
in the sketch. By Proposition
1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\Big(\mu_{m}+\eta_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+\big(\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\eta_{j_{k}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\Big)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\Big(\widetilde{\mu}_{m}+\widetilde{\eta}_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp({\alpha}_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+\big({\varphi}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{k}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\Big)d\theta.

(S.18)

We can easily construct the same concentration points for equation (
S.2.2
). Heuristically, both sides of (
S.2.2
) are multiplied by
\exp(\log n(\eta_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})+o(1)))
and
\exp(\log n(\widetilde{\eta}_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})+o(1)))
around the concentration points. Under the generic identifiability framework, we can assume that
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}),\ldots,\eta_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
are distinct. Hence we can match the two concentration points by (
S.15
). Then we can match
\lambda_{1}(t_{q+1}),\ldots,\lambda_{p}(t_{q+1})
with
\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}(t_{q+1}),\ldots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{p}(t_{q+1})
by similar method as in Case 1.

Step 2.5:
We use the same method as in Step 1.3 of Case 1 to match the rest terms.

Step 2.6:
We use the same method as in Step 1.4 of Case 1 to fix the permutation.

Case 3:
This case leads to contradiction since the summations on both side of (
S.10
) has different orders according to the discussions in Case 1 and 2.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

For simplicity, we ignore the censoring time and assume that the studying period is
[0,T]
. In the following proof, we compare the likelihood function of a given subject with given sample path on time interval
[0,T]
under two competing parametric models. Denote the intensity functions under two competing parametric models as

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}(t|X_{j},Z_{j};\theta)=\exp(\beta_{j0}+\beta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}X_{j}(t)+\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma^{1/2}A_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}Z_{j}(t))\text{,}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t|X_{j},Z_{j};\theta)=\exp(\widetilde{\beta}_{j0}+\widetilde{\beta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}X_{j}(t)+\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}{\widetilde{A}_{j}}^{\mathrm{T}}Z_{j}(t))\text{.}

where
\theta\sim\mathcal{N}_{K}(0,I_{K})
. For notation simplicity, denote
\mu_{j}(t)=\beta_{j0}+\beta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}X_{j}(t)
and
\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(t)=\widetilde{\beta}_{j0}+\widetilde{\beta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}X_{j}(t)
for
j=1,\ldots,J
. By Condition (e),
X_{j}
and
Z_{j}
are piecewise constant on
[0,T]
, which implies that
\mu_{j}
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\lambda_{j}
and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
are all piecewise constant on
[0,T]
for any
j
with probability 1. Suppose that
[0,T]
can be divided into
v
finite intervals:
[0,t_{1}],(t_{1},t_{2}],\ldots,(t_{v-1},t_{v}]
such that
X_{j}
and
Z_{j}
remain constant on each interval. We then use induction method to prove that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}
and
0\leq t\leq s\leq T
, there holds

\displaystyle{\mu}_{j}(t)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(t),

\displaystyle Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)A_{j_{1}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s)

\displaystyle=Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)\widetilde{A}_{j_{1}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s).

(S.19)

We first prove that (
S.2.2
) holds on interval
[0,t_{1}]
. Choose
t=0
in Proposition
1
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\int\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,I_{K})d\theta=\int\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,I_{K})d\theta\text{.}

(S.20)

By explicit integration of (
S.20
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]\Bigg).

(S.21)

For any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1,\ldots,J
, we introduce the following notation:
x_{j}=\exp(\mu_{j}(0))
,
\widetilde{x}_{j}=\exp(\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(0))
,
y_{j_{1}j_{2}}=\exp(\frac{1}{2}Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(0)A_{j_{1}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(0))
and
\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}=\exp(\frac{1}{2}Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(0)\widetilde{A}_{j_{1}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(0))
. If at least one of
Z_{1}(0),\ldots,Z_{J}(0)
is zero, for example
Z_{J}(0)=0
. Then by Corollary
1
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\int\exp(\mu_{J}(0))\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\Sigma)d\theta=\int\exp(\widetilde{\mu}_{J}(0))\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\widetilde{\Sigma})d\theta\text{.}

(S.22)

By (
S.20
) and (
S.22
) we have
x_{J}=\widetilde{x}_{J}
and
y_{1J}=\ldots=y_{JJ}=\widetilde{y}_{1J}=\ldots=\widetilde{y}_{JJ}=1
. Then equation (
S.2.2
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J-1}\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J-1}\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]\Bigg).

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. This means that we can still apply same analysis to the rest
J-1
event types. So we assume WLOG that
Z_{1}(0),\ldots,Z_{J}(0)
are all nonzero. Hence by excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we can assume that
\{{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}:1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq J\}
are distinct. Then by Proposition
2
, there exists permutation
\pi
:
\{1,\ldots,J\}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,J\}
such that for any
1\leq i,j\leq J
,
x_{i}=\widetilde{x}_{\pi(i)}
and
y_{ij}=\widetilde{y}_{\pi(i)\pi(j)}
. Hence
\{\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}:1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq J\}
are also distinct. On the other side, for any
j=1,\ldots,J
, Corollary
1
indicates that

\displaystyle\int\lambda_{j}(0)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\Sigma)d\theta=\int\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(0)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\widetilde{\Sigma})d\theta\text{.}

(S.23)

By explicit integration of (
S.23
), for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}(0)+\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(0)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]+\mu_{j}(0)+\frac{1}{2}Z_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}(0)A_{j}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(0)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}(0)+\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(0)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]+\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(0)+\frac{1}{2}Z_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}(0)\widetilde{A}_{j}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(0)\Bigg).

(S.24)

Divide (
S.2.2
) by (
S.2.2
) when
n=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}(0)+\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}(0)+\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(0)+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{k}}Z_{j_{k}}(0)\Big]\Bigg).

(S.25)

For any
m=1,\ldots,J
, denote

\displaystyle\psi_{m}

\displaystyle=\exp(\mu_{m}(0)+Z^{\mathrm{T}}_{m}(0)A_{m}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{m}Z_{m}(0))=x_{m}y_{mj},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{m}

\displaystyle=\exp(\widetilde{\mu}_{m}(0)+Z^{\mathrm{T}}_{m}(0)\widetilde{A}_{m}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{m}Z_{m}(0))=\widetilde{x}_{m}\widetilde{y}_{mj}.

By applying Proposition
2
to equation (
S.2.2
), there exists permutation
\widehat{\pi}:~\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}\rightarrow\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}
such that for any
1\leq i,j\leq J
,
\psi_{i}=\widetilde{\psi}_{\widehat{\pi}(i)}
and
y_{ij}=\widetilde{y}_{\widehat{\pi}(i)\widehat{\pi}(j)}
. Then for any
j=1,\ldots,J
,
\widetilde{y}_{\pi(j)\pi(j)}=\widetilde{y}_{\widehat{\pi}(j)\widehat{\pi}(j)}
.
Since
\widetilde{y}_{11},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{JJ}
are distinct,
\pi
and
\widehat{\pi}
are identical. Then

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{{\pi}(j){\pi}(j)}=y_{jj}=\frac{\psi_{j}}{x_{j}}=\frac{\widetilde{\psi}_{\pi(j)}}{\widetilde{x}_{\pi(j)}}=\widetilde{y}_{\pi(j)j}.

Since
\{\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}:1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq J\}
are distinct, we have
\pi(j)=j
. Since
j
is arbitrarily chosen from
\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}
, this implies that
\pi=id
. Hence we proved that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}
and
0\leq t\leq s\leq t_{1}
, there holds

\displaystyle{\mu}_{j}(t)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(t),

\displaystyle Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)A_{j_{1}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s)

\displaystyle=Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)\widetilde{A}_{j_{1}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s).

Now suppose that (
S.2.2
) is proved on time interval
[0,t_{q}]
. We then prove that (
S.2.2
) also holds on time interval
[0,t_{q+1}]
. Denote
t_{0}=0
and apply Proposition
1
to the case when
t=t_{q}
, we have

\displaystyle\int\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{s\leq t}\lambda_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)}\Big]\exp\Big(-\sum_{i=1}^{q}(t_{i}-t_{i-1})\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(t_{i})\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(t_{q+1})\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,I_{K})d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{s\leq t}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)}\Big]\exp\Big(-\sum_{i=1}^{q}(t_{i}-t_{i-1})\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t_{i})\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t_{q+1})\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,I_{K})d\theta\text{.}

(S.26)

To simplify the notation, for any
k=0,\ldots,q-1,~j=1\ldots,J
, define:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\varphi=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{t_{q}}\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t)dN_{j}(t),&\widetilde{\varphi}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{t_{q}}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t)dN_{j}(t),\\
\alpha_{kJ+j}=\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t_{k+1}),&\widetilde{\alpha}_{kJ+j}=\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t_{k+1}),\\
\omega_{kJ+j}=(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\exp(\mu_{j}(t_{k+1})),&\widetilde{\omega}_{kJ+j}=(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\exp(\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(t_{k+1})),\\
\eta_{j}=\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t_{q+1}),&\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t_{q+1}).\end{array}

Let
W=qJ
, then equation (
S.2.2
) can be explicitly characterized as

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}(t_{q+1})-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+\big(\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\eta_{j_{k}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\Big)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}(t_{q+1})-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\widetilde{\omega}_{k}\exp(\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+\big(\widetilde{\varphi}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{k}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\Big)d\theta.

We simplify
{\mu}_{j}(t_{q+1})
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(t_{q+1})
as
\mu_{j}
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
in the following proof. Induction assumption indicates that for any
k,k_{1},k_{2}=1,\ldots,W
,
\omega_{k}=\widetilde{\omega}_{k}
and
\alpha_{k_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k_{2}}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{k_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{k_{2}}
. Then there exists orthogonal matrix
T
in
\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
such that
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}=T\alpha_{k}
for any
k=1,\ldots,W
. So by changing variables we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mu_{j_{k}}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+\big(\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\eta_{j_{k}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\Big)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{j_{k}}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp({\alpha}_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+\big({\varphi}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}T^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{k}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\Big)d\theta.

(S.27)

For notation simplicity, we denote
T^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
as
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
. We assume WLOG that
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}
are distinct, or we can merge the identical ones together. We also assume WLOG that
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}
are nonzero, or we can eliminate the terms on both side of (
S.2.2
). For
j=1,\ldots,J
, we call that
\eta_{j}
has degenerated expansion if
Z_{j}(t_{q+1})\in span\{Z_{j}(t_{1}),\ldots,Z_{j}(t_{q})\}
. In such case, suppose that
Z_{j}(t_{q+1})=\sum_{k=1}^{q}\gamma_{k}Z_{j}(t_{k})
. Then by induction assumption, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=T^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}\big(\sum_{k=1}^{q}\gamma_{k}Z_{j}(t_{k})\big)=\sum_{k=1}^{q}\gamma_{k}T^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t_{k}))=\sum_{k=1}^{q}\gamma_{k}(\Sigma^{1/2}A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j}Z_{j}(t_{k}))=\eta_{j}.

Hence the degenerated expansion of
\eta_{j}
implies that
\eta_{j}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
.

Now we prove that there exists permutation
{\pi}:~\{1,\ldots,J\}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,J\}
such that for any
j=1,\ldots,J
,
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{\pi(j)}
and
\eta_{j}=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(j)}
. By part (1) in Proposition
4
, there exists
\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{J}},\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}},\ldots,\\
\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{J}}
which correspond to
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{J},\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{J}
. We assume WLOG that

\displaystyle\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\|=\max_{j=1,\ldots,J}\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\eta_{j}}}\eta_{j}\|,

\displaystyle\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\|=\max_{j=1,\ldots,J}\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{j}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\|.

For any
n
and
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}=(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})
, define:

\displaystyle f_{n}(\theta\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=n\mu_{1}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+(\varphi+n\eta_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(n)}\left[(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{j})\right],

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=n\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+(\varphi+n\widetilde{\eta}_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(n)}\left[(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+(\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\widetilde{\mu}_{j})\right],

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\left(f_{n}(\theta\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\right)d\theta,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\left(\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\right)d\theta,

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\binom{n}{n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(n)},\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)}}=\frac{n!}{\left(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(n)}\right)!\prod_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(n)}!}.

Furthermore, denote the unique maximizers of
{f}_{n}(\theta\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})
and
\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})
by
\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})
, respectively. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, denote
\mathcal{O}_{n}=\{(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-1}:\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}\leq n\}
. Then equation (
S.2.2
) turns into

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)}).

(S.28)

By Proposition
3
, for any
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}
we have

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\asymp\frac{\exp(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)}))}{\sqrt{\operatorname{det}(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)}))}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\asymp\frac{\exp(\widetilde{f}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)}))}{\sqrt{\operatorname{det}(-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{f}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)}))}}.

(S.29)

Since Proposition
3
implies that the ratio between both sides of (
S.2.2
) is bounded from above and away from zero, it can be ignored in the identifying procedure. The proof then falls into either of the following three cases:

Case 1:
\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\|>0
.
\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\|>0
.

Step 1:
For
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}
, we bound the denominator in (
S.2.2
) by
\exp\left(O(n)\right)
uniformly.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}
as

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}=\underset{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}{\operatorname{argmax}}~\operatorname{det}(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})).

Since
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}=O(n)
, we can prove that
\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}_{n})=O(n)
by part (2) in Proposition
4
. We can similarly define
\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
for the other side and prove that
\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{n})=O(n)
. Hence there exists
M>0
such that for any
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}
, there holds

\displaystyle 1\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big)}\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big)}\leq\exp\left(Mn\right),

\displaystyle 1\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{f}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big)}\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{f}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big)}\leq\exp\left(Mn\right).

(S.30)

Step 2:
Construct the concentration points on both side of (
S.28
).

Denote
\mathcal{G}_{0}=D=[0,1]^{J-1}
. We define
\{\mathcal{G}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
in the following inductive method: Suppose
\mathcal{G}_{k-1}
is constructed, we partition
\mathcal{G}_{k-1}
into
2^{J-1}
identical hypercubes
D_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,D_{2^{(J-1)}}^{(k)}
with length
2^{-k}
on each side. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
i=1,\ldots,2^{J-1}
, denote

\displaystyle S_{i,k,n}=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\overline{D_{i}^{(k)}}\Big).

Then we define
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\overline{D_{j_{k}}^{(k)}}
, which satisfies
S_{j_{k},k,n}=\underset{i=1,\ldots,2^{J-1}}{\max}S_{i,k,n}~~i.o.
Hence we can define a nesting hypercube sequence
\{\mathcal{G}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
. By nested interval theorem, there exists unique
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\in[0,1]^{J-1}
such that

\displaystyle(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\in\bigcap_{k=0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{G}_{k}}.

We call this point the concentration point of the left side of (
S.28
). By the definition of
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
, we have

\displaystyle S_{j_{k},k,n}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\geq 2^{-k}\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

(S.31)

infinitely often. We assume WLOG that (
S.31
) holds for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Similarly, we can define concentration point
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
for the right hand side of (
S.28
). Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{\widetilde{j}_{k},k,n}\geq 2^{-k}\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)}).

(S.32)

By (
S.28
), (
S.31
) and (
S.32
), for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\asymp\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\Big).

(S.33)

Hence we reduce equation (
S.28
) to partial sums around the two concentration points.

Step 3:
Prove that
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})=(0,\ldots,0)
.

For any
0\leq\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}\leq 1
and
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\nu_{j}\leq 1
, denote
\eta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=(1-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\nu_{j})\eta_{1}+\sum_{j=2}^{J}\nu_{j}\eta_{j}
. We first prove that
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=(0,\ldots,0)
. If this is not the case, i.e.,
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\neq(0,\ldots,0)
, then by Proposition
5
we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})}^{\perp}}\eta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\right\|<\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}\right\|.

By the continuity of canonical projection, we fix
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
large enough such that

\displaystyle\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})}^{\perp}}\eta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\right\|^{2}+\delta\leq\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}}{\min}\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})}^{\perp}}\eta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\right\|^{2}\triangleq C,

(S.34)

where
\delta>0
is constant and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
is the hypercube with length
2^{-k}
on each side which contains point
(0,\ldots,0)
. Define

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}=\underset{\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\operatorname{argmax}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

and assume that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})}{n}=\left(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J}\right)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}.

Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
), (
S.34
) and part (2) in Proposition
4
, for
n
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})=

\displaystyle\exp(o(n^{2}))\exp\left(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(o(n^{2})+\frac{n^{2}}{2}\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})}^{\perp}}\eta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(o(n^{2})+\frac{C-\delta}{2}n^{2}\right).

Hence we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}\left(\mathcal{O}_{n}\right)\exp\left(o(n^{2})+\frac{C-\delta}{2}n^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(o(n^{2})+\frac{C-\delta}{2}n^{2}\right).

(S.35)

Similarly we can prove that:

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\Big)\geq\exp\left(o(n^{2})+\frac{C}{2}n^{2}\right).

(S.36)

However, by the definition of
\left(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}\right)
, we should have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\Big)\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big),

which contradicts with (
S.2.2
) and (
S.36
). So we have
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=(0,\ldots,0)
. Similarly, we can prove that
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})=(0,\ldots,0)
.

Step 4:
Separate the order of summation on both sides of (
S.33
).

For
0\leq\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}\leq 1
such that
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\nu_{j}\leq 1
, by part (2) of Proposition
4
we can define

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}(n\nu_{2},\ldots,n\nu_{J})}{n}\triangleq\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}).

Now we rank
\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0}),j=2,\ldots,J
, in decreasing order. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we can assume WLOG that there are no ties and
\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})>\ldots>\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{J}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
. By part (2) in Proposition
4
,
\theta(\mathbf{0})=P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}
. Then by Proposition
5
we have

\displaystyle\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{J}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})=\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{J}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}\triangleq\delta>0.

For any
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-1}
, denote
T(\bm{\xi})=-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
. Then we rank all the components in
\{T(\bm{\xi}):\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-1}\}
in decreasing order. For any
r\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
\bm{\xi}^{(r)}
be the array such that the rank of
T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})
is
r
. Suppose the rank of
T(0,\ldots,0,1,1)
is
r^{\ast}
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we can assume that there are no ties among
T(\bm{\xi}^{(1)}),\ldots,T(\bm{\xi}^{(r^{\ast}+1)})
. By the continuity of canonical projection proved in Proposition
4
, we fix
k
large enough such that

\displaystyle\underset{j=2,\ldots,J}{\min}\underset{(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}.

(S.37)

Now we fix
r
such that
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
. By part (2) in Proposition
4
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})}{n}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})}{n}=P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}=\theta(\mathbf{0}).

(S.38)

Then by (
S.38
), for any
\widetilde{r}\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({\widetilde{r}})}\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})=\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({\widetilde{r}})}\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})=T(\bm{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}).

(S.39)

By (
S.38
), there also holds

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\big)}{\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\big)}=\exp\left(o(n)\right).

(S.40)

Moreover, by Stirling formula we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})}\asymp

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r+1)}}{n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}}}\exp\Big[n\log n-\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r+1)}\big)\log\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r+1)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-n\log n+\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}\big)\log\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\exp\big(\big[\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r+1)}-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}\big]\log n\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(o(n)).

(S.41)

By (
S.2.2
), (
S.39
), (
S.40
) and (
S.2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(o(n))\frac{\exp\big(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\big)}{\exp\big(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(o(n)-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}\left[\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r+1)}\left[\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{j})\right]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\big(o(n)-n\left[T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})-T(\bm{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})})\right]\big).

(S.42)

Now we prove that
\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})
is the largest one among all terms with rank lower than
r+1
when
n
is large enough. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, suppose

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}=\underset{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\setminus\{\bm{\xi}^{(l)}:l=1,\ldots,r\}}{\operatorname{argmax}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}).

If
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
is unbounded, we assume WLOG that
\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}\rightarrow\infty
and suppose that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}/n=\left(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J}\right)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}
. Then by part (2) of Proposition
4
, we have
\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})/n\rightarrow\theta\left(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J}\right)
. Since it is easy to show that
\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})-\theta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})=O(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big)\asymp\operatorname{det}\big(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\big).

(S.43)

Then by (
S.37
) and (
S.43
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})}\gtrsim

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})}\frac{\exp\left(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\right)}{\exp\left(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\right)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\exp\left(o(n)+\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}_{n})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\exp\left(o(n)+n\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\infty,

which contradicts with the definition of
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
since unboundedness of
\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}
implies that
(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\notin\left\{\bm{\xi}^{(l)}:l=1,\ldots,r\right\}
when
n
is large. Hence
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
is bounded. Then by similar argument as (
S.2.2
), it is easy to see that
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}=\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)}
for
n
large enough. So for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
, by (
S.2.2
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{u\geq r+1}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(u)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(u)})\mathbf{1}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(u)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\right\}}{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})}\lesssim

\displaystyle n^{J}\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle n^{J}\exp\big(o(n)-n\left[T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})-T(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)})\right]\big)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0

(S.44)

when
k
is large enough. Similarly, we assume WLOG that
(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{2})^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\mathbf{0})>\ldots>(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{J})^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\mathbf{0})
and define
\widetilde{T}(\bm{\xi})
. Moreover, we assume WLOG that the rank of
\widetilde{T}(0,\ldots,0,1,1)
is no greater than
r^{\ast}
. Then we can similarly prove that for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
and
k
large enough,

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{u\geq r+1}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(u)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(u)})\mathbf{1}\{\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(u)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\}}{\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)})}\rightarrow 0.

(S.45)

Step 5:
Prove that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1,\ldots,J
,
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
.

We use induction method to prove that for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
, there holds
\bm{\xi}^{(r)}=\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)}
,
T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})=\widetilde{T}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})
and
\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})=\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)})
.

For
r=1
, by (
S.33
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.45
) we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(1)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(1)})\asymp\sum_{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi})\mathbf{I}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\asymp\sum_{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi})\mathbf{I}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\asymp\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(1)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(1)}).

(S.46)

It is easy to see that
{\bm{\xi}}^{(1)}=\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(1)}=\mathbf{0}
, so we have
\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})\asymp\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})
. We have

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})=

\displaystyle\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\left(n\mu_{1}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+(\varphi+n\eta_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|nP_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi\right\|^{2}+n\mu_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\left(-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+n\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\theta^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}}(\varphi+n\eta_{1})-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta\right)d\theta.

(S.47)

Now define

\displaystyle f_{n}(\theta)=

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+n\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi)+\theta^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}}(\varphi+n\eta_{1})-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta)=

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+n\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}+\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi)+\theta^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}(\varphi+n\widetilde{\eta}_{1})-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta

and denote the unique maximizer of
f_{n}
by
\widehat{\theta}_{n}
. By Proposition
3
, we have

\displaystyle\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp\left(f_{n}(\theta)\right)d\theta\asymp\frac{\exp(f_{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}))}{\sqrt{\operatorname{det}(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}))}}.

(S.48)

By Proposition
4
, there exists
\left\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\right\}\subseteq\left\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{K}\right\}
such that
P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}}\eta=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}\alpha_{k_{j}}
. By Lemma
2
, we can assume WLOG that
\left\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\right\}
are linearly independent. Then by similar method as in the proof of Lemma
4
, we can prove that
\widehat{\theta}_{n}/\log n\rightarrow\widehat{\theta}\in\text{span}\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\}
and
\alpha_{k_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}=\ldots=\alpha_{k_{m}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}=1
. Denote
\widehat{\theta}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{k_{j}}\alpha_{k_{j}}
. Then
\delta=(\delta_{k_{1}},\ldots,\delta_{k_{m}})
is the unique solution of linear equation

\displaystyle(\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})^{\mathrm{T}}(\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})\delta=\textbf{1}_{k}.

The denominator in the right hand side of (
S.48
) has order
\exp(O(\log n))
. Then by similar method as in the proof of Lemma
4
, we expand
\log\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})
in decreasing order as

\displaystyle\log\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})=

\displaystyle n^{2}\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}\right\|/2+n\log n\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}+n\left[(P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}\log\frac{\gamma_{k_{j}}}{\omega_{k_{j}}}+\mu_{1}\right]

\displaystyle-\log^{2}n\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta^{2}_{k_{j}}/2+o(1)\right).

Similarly we can prove that

\displaystyle\log\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})=

\displaystyle n^{2}\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\right\|/2+n\log n\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}}+n\left[(P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi-\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}}\log\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}}}{\omega_{k_{j}}}+\widetilde{\mu}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle-\log^{2}n\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}_{k_{j}}/2+o(1)\right).

Since
\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})\asymp\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})
by (
S.46
), we can match the coefficients of each term. In particular, we have

\displaystyle 1_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\left((\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})^{\mathrm{T}}(\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})\right)^{-1}1_{m}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta^{2}_{k_{j}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}_{k_{j}}=1_{\widetilde{m}}^{\mathrm{T}}\left((\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\alpha}_{k_{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{\alpha}_{k_{m}})\right)^{-1}1_{\widetilde{m}}.

By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we can assume that among all choices (finite choices) of linearly independent subset
\left\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\right\}\subseteq\left\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\right\}
, the values of
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta^{2}_{k_{j}}=1_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\left((\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})^{\mathrm{T}}(\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}})\right)^{-1}1_{m}
are distinct. Then
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta^{2}_{k_{j}}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{\delta}^{2}_{k_{j}}
implies that
\alpha_{k_{1}}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{k_{m}}
and
\delta_{k_{1}}=\widetilde{\delta}_{k_{1}},\ldots,\delta_{k_{m}}=\widetilde{\delta}_{k_{m}}
.
By the proof in Step 4, it is easy to see that there exists constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})-\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})}{\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})}\lesssim\exp(-Cn),

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})-\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})}{\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})}\lesssim\exp(-Cn).

Since
\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})
, we have
|\log\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})-\log\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})|\lesssim\exp(-Cn)
. Now we match the terms with lower order. If we look at all terms with order no less than
O(\log n)
, we have

\displaystyle\log\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})=

\displaystyle n^{2}\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}\right\|/2+n\log n\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}+n\left[(P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}\log\frac{\gamma_{k_{j}}}{\omega_{k_{j}}}+\mu_{1}\right]

\displaystyle-\log^{2}n\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta^{2}_{k_{j}}/2+\log n\left(-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{k_{j}}\log\frac{\gamma_{k_{j}}}{\omega_{k_{j}}}-\frac{m}{2}\right)+o(\log n).

Similar expansion is also obtained for
\log\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})
. Then by matching coefficients, we can derive

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{k_{j}}\log\frac{\gamma_{k_{j}}}{\omega_{k_{j}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\delta_{k_{j}}\log\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}}}{\omega_{k_{j}}}.

Following similar arguments as in
(
Shun and McCullagh, 1995
)
, we expand
\log\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})
and
\log\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})
into infinite series and match the coefficients of terms with order
n^{-l_{1}}\log^{l_{2}}n
where
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and derive similar equations regarding
(\gamma_{k_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{k_{m}})
and
(\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{m}})
. By these equations we can match each coefficient:
\gamma_{k_{1}}=\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{k_{m}}=\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{m}}
. Hence we have

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\gamma_{k_{j}}\alpha_{k_{j}}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{\gamma}_{k_{j}}\alpha_{k_{j}}=P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}

and
\mu_{1}=\widetilde{\mu}_{1}
. Moreover, for
j=1,\ldots,m
we have

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{1}=

\displaystyle\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}\|^{2}+\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\right\|^{2}=\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\|^{2}+\left\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\right\|^{2}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1},

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k_{j}}=

\displaystyle\left(P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k_{j}}=\left(P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k_{j}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k_{j}}.

Now we should match the inner product between
P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}
,
P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
and vectors in
\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\}\setminus\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\}
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we can assume that
\alpha_{k}
is the unique vector among
\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\}\setminus\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\}
such that
\alpha_{k}=\operatorname{argmax}_{\alpha\in\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\}\setminus\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\}}\\
\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|nP_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi\right\|^{2}+n\mu_{1}\right)\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{D}{2}}\exp\left(f_{n}(\theta)\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|nP_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi\right\|^{2}+n\mu_{1}\right)\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{D}{2}}\exp\left(g_{n}(\theta)\right)d\theta

\displaystyle+\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|nP_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi\right\|^{2}+n\mu_{1}-n\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+o(n)\right)\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{D}{2}}\exp\left(g_{n}(\theta)\right)d\theta

Note that we can easily prove that

\displaystyle\log\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|nP_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi\right\|^{2}+n\mu_{1}\right)\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{D}{2}}\exp\left(g_{n}(\theta)\right)d\theta

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\log\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|nP_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\varphi\right\|^{2}+n\mu_{1}-n\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+o(n)\right)\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{D}{2}}\exp\left(g_{n}(\theta)\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})+o(n).

This implies that we should also match those remainder terms. Moreover, if the first order remainder terms are matched on both sides, then the higher order remainder terms are also matched. Hence we insert all first order remainder terms into the ranking
\left\{T(\bm{\xi}):\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-1}\right\}
with value indexed by
-\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
for all
\alpha\in\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\}\setminus\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\}
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we assume that there are no ties in the ranking. Then we can still match the term in the ranking in decreasing order. The new added remainder terms are matched with the remainder terms on the right hand side in a similar fashion. For simplicity, we assume that all the first-order remainder terms has ranks higher than
T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})
. Then by matching order in similar way, we can prove that for any
\alpha\in\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\}\setminus\{\alpha_{k_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{k_{m}}\}
, there holds

\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}=\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{1}=\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}+\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}=\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}^{\perp}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}+\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}=\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}.

Now we have proved that
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
for
k=1,\ldots,W
. Then we can easily see that
\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})=\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})
. So the result is proved for
r=1
.

If the case is proved for
1,\ldots,r-1
, then by (
S.28
) and induction assumption,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 1}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})-\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 1}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})-\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)}).

(S.49)

We then use the same construction method as in Step 2 to define the concentration points for both sides of (
S.2.2
) and use the same method as in Step 3 to prove that the concentration points for both sides of (
S.2.2
) are also
(0,\ldots,0)
. So for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})\mathbf{I}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\asymp

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(l)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq r}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big).

(S.50)

Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.45
) and (
S.2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})\asymp\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)}).

(S.51)

Then by similar method as in the proof of Proposition
2
, we can match
\bm{\xi}^{(r)}
with
\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)}
and match
T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})
with
\widetilde{T}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})
. Then by similar proof as in the case
r=1
, we can match all cross terms and prove that
\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})=\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)})
. By induction method, we can prove that for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
, there holds
\bm{\xi}^{(r)}=\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)}
,
T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})=\widetilde{T}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})
and
\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(r)})=\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)})
. For any
j=1,\ldots,J
, choose
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
be the array such that

\displaystyle\xi_{m}=\begin{cases}1~~~&m=j\\
0~~~&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

It is easy to see that the rank of
\bm{\xi}
is higher than
r^{\ast}
. In the inductive proof, we matched all cross terms in
\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi})
, i.e.,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
for any
k=1,\ldots,W
and
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
. Moreover we have
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
. For any
1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}\leq J
, choose
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
be the array such that

\displaystyle\xi_{m}=\begin{cases}1~~~&m=j_{1}\text{ or }j_{2}\\
0~~~&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

The rank of
\bm{\xi}
is higher than
r^{\ast}
, by matching all cross terms, we have proved that
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
.

Step 6:
Fix the permutation.

Due to the purpose of notation simplicity, we permute the order of subscript
\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}
on both sides of (
S.28
) in the previous steps. So far, we have only proved that there exists permutation
{\pi}:~\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}\rightarrow\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}
such that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1,\ldots,J
and
k=1,\ldots,W
, there hold
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{\pi(j)}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(j)}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(j_{1})}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(j_{2})}
.

We then prove that for any
j=1,\ldots,J
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
. If this is not the case, we assume WLOG that
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})>\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
. We redefine
\widetilde{f}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{n}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})=

\displaystyle n\widetilde{\mu}_{\pi(1)}-\sum_{k=1}^{W}\omega_{k}\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta)+(\varphi+n\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(1)})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(n)}\left[(\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(1)}-\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(j)})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta+(\widetilde{\mu}_{\pi(1)}-\widetilde{\mu}_{\pi(j)})\right]-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\mathrm{T}}\theta,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})=

\displaystyle\int(2\pi)^{-\frac{K}{2}}\exp(\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta|\bm{\xi}^{(n)}))d\theta.

Then
\widetilde{f}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{n}
match with the notation in previous step. There exists
l\in\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}
such that
\pi(l)=j
. By Corollary
1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{l}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)}).

Similarly we can prove that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{l}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big).

(S.52)

Moreover, we have proved in Steps 1-5 that

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)\asymp\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big).

(S.53)

By the continuity of
\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
, we fix
k
large enough such that

\displaystyle\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})>\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J}).

(S.54)

For any
n
, we define

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}=\underset{\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\operatorname{argmin}}\frac{\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}.

Furthermore, assume WLOG that
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}/n\rightarrow(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}
. So we have

\displaystyle\varliminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\geq\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J}).

(S.55)

Moreover, it is easy to see that
\theta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})=O(1)
and

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n+1}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)}\right)\asymp

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n+1}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}\right).

(S.56)

By (
S.2.2
), (
S.55
) and (
S.56
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{n+1}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})}{\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\left(f_{n+1}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(n)}\right)}{\exp\big(f_{n+1}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}\big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\big(o(n)+n\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\big).

(S.57)

Similarly, for
n
large enough we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{l}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\big(o(n)+n\underset{(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}{\theta}(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{J})\big).

(S.58)

Then (
S.2.2
), (
S.53
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.2.2
) lead to contradiction. So for any
j=1,\ldots,J
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
. Note that the proof in Step 5 indicates that
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi_{j}}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
and
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0}),\ldots,\eta_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\mathbf{0})
are distinct. So we have
\pi=id
and
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,J
and
k=1,\ldots,W
. Hence the result is proved on
[0,t_{q+1}]
.

Case 2:
\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\|=\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\|=0
.

In this case,
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{J},\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{J}\in{X}\triangleq\{\sum_{k=1}^{W}\gamma_{k}\alpha_{k}:\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{W}\geq 0\}
by Proposition
4
. By Proposition
6
, for any
j=1,\ldots,J
, there exists canonical expansions for
\eta_{j}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
under
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}
as:
\eta_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{j}}\gamma_{j,k}\alpha_{j,k}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{j}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{j,k}\widetilde{\alpha}_{j,k}
, where the canonical expansion is unique in the sense that
\sum_{k=1}^{m_{j}}\gamma_{j,k}
,
\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{j}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{j,k}
are uniquely determined for each
j=1,\ldots,J
.

We assume WLOG that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}

\displaystyle=\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\max}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{j}}\gamma_{j,k},

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,k}

\displaystyle=\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\max}\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{j}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{j,k}.

We first discuss the case where
\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}
and
\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,k}
are the unique maximizers, respectively.

Step 1:
For
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}
, we bound the denominator part in (
S.2.2
) by
\exp\left(O(\log n)\right)
uniformly.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}^{(n)}=\underset{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}{\operatorname{argmax}}\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\big|\bm{\xi})\right).

Denote
l_{n}=\|\theta_{n}(\bar{\xi}^{(n)})\|
and
\epsilon_{n}=\theta_{n}(\bar{\xi}^{(n)})/l_{n}\rightarrow\epsilon
. If
l_{n}
is bounded, then it is easy to see that
\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\right)
is also bounded. If
l_{n}
is not bounded, we assume WLOG that
l_{n}\rightarrow\infty
. Since
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{J}\in X
, we can use the same proof as in Proposition
8
to show that
l_{n}=O(\log n)
. So there exists
\widetilde{M}>0
such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle{\max}_{k=1,\ldots,W}\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\leq\widetilde{M}\log n.

Similarly, we define
\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
and perform the same argument. Then there exists
M>0
such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}
, there holds

\displaystyle 1\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\right)}\leq n^{M},

\displaystyle 1\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{f}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\big|\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{f}_{n}(\widetilde{\theta}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\big|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\right)}\leq n^{M}.

(S.59)

Step 2:
Construct the concentration points on both side of (
S.28
).

We define
\log 0=0
. For notation simplicity, for
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}=(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})
, denote
\log\bm{\xi}^{(n)}=(\log\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\log\xi_{J}^{(n)})
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{0}=[0,1]^{J-1}
. We define
\left\{\mathcal{G}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\right\}
in the following inductive method: Suppose
\mathcal{G}_{k-1}
is constructed, we partition
\mathcal{G}_{k-1}
into
2^{J-1}
identical hypercubes
D_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,D_{2^{(J-1)}}^{(k)}
with length
2^{-k}
on each side. For any
n
and
i=1,\ldots,2^{J-1}
, denote

\displaystyle S_{i,k,n}=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{\log n}\log\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\overline{D_{i}^{(k)}}\Big).

We define
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\overline{D_{j_{k}}^{(k)}}
, which satisfies
S_{j_{k},k,n}=\underset{i=1,\ldots,2^{J-1}}{\max}S_{i,k,n}
i.o. Then there exists unique
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\in[0,1]^{J-1}
such that

\displaystyle(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\in\bigcap_{k=0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{G}_{k}}.

We call this point the concentration point of the left side of (
S.28
). Similarly we can define concentration point
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
for the right side of (
S.28
) and the corresponding hypercube sequence
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
for the right side of (
S.28
). For notation simplicity, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\left\{1\right\}
, define
\mathcal{E}_{k,n}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k,n}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k,n}=

\displaystyle\big\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}:\frac{1}{\log n}\log\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\big\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k,n}=

\displaystyle\big\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}:\frac{1}{\log n}\log\bm{\xi}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\big\}.

Similar to Step 2 in Case 1, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\asymp

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\asymp\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)}).

(S.60)

Step 3:
Characterize
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
and
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
.

Similar to Steps 3-5 in Case 1, we need the continuity property within the neighborhood of the two concentration points. While the continuity in Case 1 holds without any conditions based on Proposition
4
, we need to verify two things in order to ensure the continuity in Case 2 according to Proposition
8
. First, we need to verify the nondegeneracy condition which is needed in Proposition
8
. However, the non-degeneracy condition is itself proved reversely by equation (
S.68
) that we want to obtain in Step 3, which is hard to prove without the continuity property. This urges us to shift our focus from treating the whole summation within hypercubes to treating the term at a single point. Moreover, we need to show that for
j
such that
\nu_{j}=0
,
\eta_{j}
should appear only finite times in the dominant terms in the left side of (
S.28
).

To overcome these difficulties, the sketch of Step 3 is as follows:

(1)

We define the single point
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}
which achieves the largest summation
\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})
in each
\mathcal{E}_{k,n}
and obtain the limiting point
({\nu}_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,{\nu}_{J}^{(k)})
in each hypercube
\mathcal{G}_{k}
.

(2)

For any
j
such that
\nu_{j}^{(k)}>0
, we obtain the equation on
\eta_{j}
and
\nu_{j}^{(k)}
based on the maximum property.

(3)

We construct generalized characterization equation for
\widehat{\theta}_{k}
.

(4)

For any
j
such that
\nu_{j}^{(k)}=0
, we prove the inequality for such on
\eta_{j}
and
\nu_{j}^{(k)}
based on the maximum property. Then we exclude the cases where the occurrence of
\eta_{j}
among
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}
is nonzero for any fixed
k
in the generalized characterization equation.

(5)

We verify that the generalized characterization equation obtained in the previous step is a characterization equation. Since
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\in\bigcap_{k=0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{G}_{k}}
, the limiting point
({\nu}_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,{\nu}_{J}^{(k)})
should also converge to
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
as
k
goes to infinity. Then the characterization equations at
({\nu}_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,{\nu}_{J}^{(k)})
should converge to characterization equation at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
, which verify the nondegeneracy condition. This implies that
\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\theta(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{J}^{(k)})
.

(6)

Finally, by the continuity property, the equality and inequality also converge to equality and inequality at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
. The equality case in the inequality is eliminated after excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space.

We first characterize
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
. We assume WLOG that
\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p}>0
and
\nu_{p+1}=\ldots=\nu_{J}=0
. For any
k,n\in\mathbb{N}
, denote

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}=\underset{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}{\operatorname{argmax}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}).

For any fixed
k
, by similar method as in the proof of Proposition
4
, we can prove that
\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})=O(\log n)
. Then we denote

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}{\log n}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\theta}_{k},

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}}{\log n}

\displaystyle=(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{J}^{(k)}).

Since
(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{J}^{(k)})\in\mathcal{G}_{k}
, we have
(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{J}^{(k)})\rightarrow(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
as
k
goes to infinity by the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{k}
. We assume WLOG that
\nu_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{p_{1}}^{(k)}>0
and
\nu_{p_{1}+1}^{(k)}=\ldots=\nu_{J}^{(k)}=0
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, where
1\leq p\leq p_{1}\leq J
.

Step 3.1:
We first prove that
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\ll n
by method of contradiction. If this is not the case, then
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\asymp n
. We assume WLOG that
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\geq\delta n
for
n
large where
\delta
is a positive constant. By the proof in Proposition
4
, we have
\max_{k=1,\ldots,K}\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\mathbf{0})\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(o(n\log n))\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\mathbf{0})\phi_{n}(\mathbf{0})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\Big(o(n\log n)+\log n\big[\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{j}}\gamma_{j,k}+\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\big)\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}\big]\Big)}{\exp\Big(n\log n\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\Big(o(n\log n)+n\log n\big[\delta\max_{j=2,\ldots,J}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{j}}\gamma_{j,k}+(1-\delta)\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}-\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(o(n\log n)+\delta n\log n\big[\max_{j=2,\ldots,J}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{j}}\gamma_{j,k}-\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0,

which contradicts with (
S.2.2
).

Step 3.2:
We then prove that for any
j=1,\ldots,p_{1}
, there holds

\displaystyle 1-\nu_{j}^{(k)}=(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}.

If this is not the case for
j
, we first discuss the case when
1-\nu_{j}^{(k)}<(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}
. Suppose
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}-(1-\nu_{j}^{(k)})=\delta
, where
\delta>0
is a constant. We choose
k
large enough and fix
\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}=\nu_{j}^{(k)}-\delta/2
. Denote
\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}=(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)},\ldots,n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)})
. By Stirling formula, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}\asymp

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)})\prod_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}}{(n-\sum_{l\neq j}\bar{\xi}_{l}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}\prod_{l\neq j}\bar{\xi}_{l}^{(k,n)}}}

(S.61)

\displaystyle\times\exp\Big(-\big(n-\underset{l\neq j}{\sum}\xi_{l}{(n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}\big)\log\big(n-\underset{l\neq j}{\sum}\xi_{l}^{(n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}\big)

\displaystyle-\underset{l\neq j}{\sum}\bar{\xi}_{l}^{(k,n)}\log\bar{\xi}_{l}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}\log n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}+\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\big)\log\big(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\big)+\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\log\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\Big)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}}{n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}}}\exp\big(-(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})\log n+(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})\log n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\big(o(\log n)-(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})(1-\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)})\log n\big).

(S.62)

Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.61
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle n^{-M}\frac{\exp\big(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\big|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\big)}{\exp\big(f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\big|\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\big)}\exp\big(o(\log n)-(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})(1-\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)})\log n\big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(O(\log n)+(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})\left[(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{j})\right]-(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})(1-\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)})\log n\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(O(\log n)+(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})\log n\big[(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}-(1-\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)})\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\delta}{2}(\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}})\log n\right).

(S.63)

Since
\log\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}/\log n\rightarrow{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}
, there holds
n^{\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)}}\ll\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}
for
n
large enough. Suppose
({\nu}_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{j}^{(k)},\\
\ldots,{\nu}_{J}^{(k)})
belongs to hypercube
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
with length
2^{-k}
on each side. Suppose that
k
is chosen large enough, then we have
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\neq{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
. So by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
) and the definition of
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{\log n}\log\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\Big)}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\mathbf{1}\Big(\frac{1}{\log n}\log\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in{\mathcal{G}}_{k}\Big)}\geq n^{-J}\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}\gg 1,

which contradicts with the construction method of
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
. If
\nu_{j}^{(k)}<1
, then we can use the same method to prove the other side. If
\nu_{j}^{(k)}=1
, we have
(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)})\in\mathcal{E}_{n,k}
since
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\ll n
. Then we can similarly prove that

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}+1,\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}\gg 1

if
1-\nu_{j}^{(k)}<(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}
. This contradicts with the definition of
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}
. Hence we proved that for any
j=1,\ldots,p_{1}
and any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\nu_{j}^{(k)}=(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}.

(S.64)

Step 3.3:
Similar to Step 3.2, we can prove that for any
j=p_{1}+1,\ldots,J
,
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}\geq 1
. We assume WLOG that for any
j=p_{1}+1,\ldots,p_{4}
, there holds
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
, for any
j=p_{4}+1,\ldots,J
, there holds
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}>1
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover, for
j=p_{1}+1,\ldots,p_{4}
, we assume WLOG that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}}{\log n}=\begin{cases}+\infty&\text{ for }j=p_{1}+1,\ldots,p_{2}\\
\widehat{c}_{j,k}\in(0,\infty)&\text{ for }j=p_{2}+1,\ldots,p_{3}\\
0&\text{ for }j=p_{3}+1,\ldots,p_{4}\end{cases}.

For
j=p_{4}+1,\ldots,J
, we can easily prove that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds
\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}=0
for
n
large enough..

For any fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we use similar method as in the proof of Lemma
4
to create a linear equation for
\widehat{\theta}_{k}
. The same notations as in Lemma
4
is used. If
\nu_{2}^{(k)}>\ldots>\nu_{p_{1}}^{(k)}
, then we can use exactly the same method to set up equations for
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{p_{1}}
and
(\nu_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{p_{1}}^{(k)})
. If there exists tie among
(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{p_{1}}^{(k)})
, for example
\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}>\nu_{4}
, then the procedure falls into the following two cases:

Case 1:
If
\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}\gg\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(k,n)}
or
\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(k,n)}\gg\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}
, we assume WLOG that the first case holds. Then we project the first order equation on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and divide both side by
\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}
:

\displaystyle-\sum_{k:\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{1}}\frac{\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k})}{\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp}}\alpha_{k}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp}}\eta_{2}=o(1).

So the same method can be performed to obtain the expansion of
\eta_{2}
and
\eta_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
. Then we project the first order equation on
\mathcal{H}_{2}
and divide both side by
\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(k,n)}
to obtain the expansion of
\eta_{3}
and
\eta_{3}\in\mathcal{H}_{3}
.

Case 2:
If
\lim_{n\rightarrow}\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(k,n)}/\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}=c\in\left(0,\infty\right)
, then we project the first order equation on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
and divide both side by
\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}
:

\displaystyle-\sum_{k:\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{1}}\frac{\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k})}{\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp}}\alpha_{k}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\perp}}\left(\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3}\right)=o(1).

By same method we obtain
\mathcal{H}_{2}
and
\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
. If the expansion of
\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3}
is the sum of the degenerated expansions of
\eta_{2}
and
\eta_{3}
, then the nondegeneracy condition in Proposition
8
is satisfied. If this is not the case, then for any
\widehat{c}
in a small neighborhood of
c
, we consider
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})}=(\xi_{2}^{(n,\widehat{c})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n,\widehat{c})})
such that

\displaystyle\xi_{2}^{(n,\widehat{c})}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\widehat{c}}\left(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}+\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(k,n)}\right),

\displaystyle\xi_{3}^{(n,\widehat{c})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{c}}{1+\widehat{c}}\left(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)}+\bar{\xi}_{3}^{(k,n)}\right),

\displaystyle\xi_{j}^{(n,\widehat{c})}=

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)},j=4,\ldots,J.

We then construct characterization equation at
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})}
in a similar method. There are finite many choice of
\widehat{c}
in the neighborhood of
c
such that
\eta_{2}+\widehat{c}\eta_{3}
can be spanned by less than
K-1
linearly independent vectors in
\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{K}\}
. Hence we assume WLOG that for any
\widehat{c}>c
in the neighborhood of
c
,
\eta_{2}+\widehat{c}\eta_{3}
are spanned by the same basis
A
. For any
\widehat{c}<c
in the neighborhood of
c
,
\eta_{2}+\widehat{c}\eta_{3}
are spanned by the same basis
\widetilde{A}
.

Case 2.1:
We first consider the case where the coefficients in the expansion of
\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3}
under
A
and
\widetilde{A}
both contain zero component. Note that
A\neq\widetilde{A}
, otherwise the linear dependency between expansion coefficients and
\widehat{c}
will imply that the coefficient has negative components on one side, which contradicts with the construction method of characterization equation. Then this implies that
A^{-1}(\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3})
and
\widetilde{A}^{-1}(\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3})
both contains at least one zero entry. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, this can not happen.

Case 2.2:
If the coefficients in the expansion of
\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3}
under either
X
or
\widetilde{X}
are all nonzero. Then by the continuity of coefficients in the expansion with respect to
\widehat{c}
, we have
X=\widetilde{X}
in the small neighborhood of
c
. By similar method as in the proof of Lemma
4
, we can expand
\log\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})
in decreasing order, it is easy to verify that the coefficient of term
n^{\nu_{2}}\log n
depends linearly on
c
. On the other hand, we can easily seen from Stirling formula that the terms in
\log\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})
which depends on
\widehat{c}
has smaller order than
n^{\nu_{2}}\log n
by the definition of
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})}
. Hence by the maximum property of
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,c)}=\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}
, the linear coefficient of
n^{\nu_{2}}\log n
should be equal to zero, which leads to contradiction when a zero measure set in the parameter space is excluded.

By the above discussion, expansion of
\eta_{2}+c\eta_{3}
is the sum of the degenerated expansions of
\eta_{2}
and
\eta_{3}
, then the nondegeneracy condition in Proposition
8
is satisfied. So we can construct the linear equation in the same way as in Case 1.

Now we have constructed
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{p_{1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{p_{1}}
. For
j=p_{1}+1,\ldots,p_{2}
