, since
\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\gg\log n
, the linear equation is created in the same way as in
j=1,\ldots,p_{1}
. So we can obtain
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{p_{2}}\in\mathcal{H}_{p_{2}}
.

For
j=p_{3}+1,\ldots,J
, since
\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\ll\log n
, the term containing
\eta_{j}
vanishes when dividing the first order equation by
\log n
.

Now we project the first order equation on
\mathcal{H}_{p_{2}}
and divide both side by
\log n
, we will get:

\displaystyle-\sum_{k:\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{E}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{p_{2}}}\frac{\exp(\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k})}{\log n}P_{\mathcal{H}_{p_{2}}^{\perp}}\alpha_{k}+=P_{\mathcal{H}_{p_{2}}^{\perp}}\Big(\widehat{\theta}_{k}-\sum_{j=p_{2}+1}^{p_{3}}\widehat{c}_{j,k}\eta_{j}\Big)+o(1).

So we require
\widehat{\theta}_{k}-\sum_{j=p_{2}+1}^{p_{3}}\widehat{c}_{j,k}\eta_{j}
to be spanned by the basis in the linear equation, which has a unique solution
\widehat{\theta}_{k}
. We call it a generalized characterization equation for
\widehat{\theta}_{k}
at
(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{J}^{(k)})
.

Step 3.4:
Now we prove that the constructed generalized characterization equation is a valid characterization equation. Moreover, there holds
p=p_{4}
, i.e., all four parts in
p+1,\ldots,p_{4}
are excluded.

Part 1:
For
j=p+1,\ldots,p_{1}
, we assume WLOG that for any
k
,
\eta_{j}
is expanded by the same vectors in
\left\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{W}\right\}
, then we have
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}1-\nu_{j}^{(k)}=1
since
(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{J}^{(k)})\rightarrow(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
. By the construction method of generalized characterization equation, this cannot happen outside a zero measure set in the parameter space. So we have
p=p_{1}
.

Part 2:
For
j=p_{1}+1,\ldots,p_{2}
, since
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p_{1}+1})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\ldots=(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p_{2}})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
implies that
\eta_{p_{1}+1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\ldots=\eta_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}
, by excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, the expansion of
\eta_{\widehat{p}+1},\ldots,\eta_{p_{1}}
under the basis in the generalized characterization equation should all be degenerated, which indicate that
\eta_{p_{1}+1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\ldots=\eta_{p_{2}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=0
since
\nu_{p_{1}+1}=\ldots=\nu_{p_{2}}=0
. Then
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, this cannot happen. So we have
p_{1}=p_{2}
.

Part 3:
For
j=p_{3}+1,\ldots,p_{4}
, by the construction method,
\eta_{j}
is not involved in the generalized characterization equation. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space,
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
cannot happen. So we have
p_{3}=p_{4}
.

Part 4:
For arbitrary fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have already proved that for
j=p+1,\ldots,p_{4}
,
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}}/{\log n}=\widehat{c}_{j,k}\in(0,\infty)
. Denote
\mathcal{H}=span\{\alpha_{k}:k=1,\ldots,W,\alpha_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}\geq 0\}
.

Case 1:
If the generalized characterization equation contains at least one type-2 equation, then at least one of
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{p}
has nondegenerated expansion in the characterization equation. This implies that
\eta_{p+1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k},\ldots,\eta_{p_{4}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}
does not depend on the value of
\widehat{c}_{p+1,k},\ldots,\widehat{c}_{p_{4},k}
. Then by excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space,
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p+1})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\ldots=(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p_{4}})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
cannot happen.

Case 2:
If the characterization equation contains type-1 equations only. Then the conditions in Proposition
7
is satisfied. Denote
\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})}=(\bar{\xi}_{2}^{(k,n)},\ldots,\bar{\xi}_{p}^{(k,n)},\widehat{\xi}_{p+1}^{(n,{c}_{p+1})},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{p_{4}}^{(n,{c}_{p_{4}})},0,\ldots,0)
where

\displaystyle\widehat{\xi}_{j}^{(n,{c}_{j})}=\frac{c_{j}}{\widehat{c}_{j,k}}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)},j=p+1,\ldots,p_{4}.

By Proposition
7
, there exists
D_{n,1},D_{2}
which does not depend on
c
such that for
c=(c_{p+1},\ldots,c_{p_{4}})
in a small neighborhood of
\widehat{c}=(\widehat{c}_{p+1,k},\ldots,\widehat{c}_{p_{4},k})
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})\big|\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})=o(\log^{2}n)+D_{n,1}+\log^{2}n\left(c^{\mathrm{T}}D_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}{c}_{j}P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{j}\right\|^{2}\right).

(S.65)

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})\big|\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})\right)}{\operatorname{det}\left(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})\big|\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})\right)}=\exp\left(o(\log^{2}n)\right).

(S.66)

By Stirling formula, we can similarly prove that

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})}{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})}=

\displaystyle\exp\left(o(\log^{2}n)+\log^{2}n\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}\left(c_{j}-\widehat{c}_{j,k}\right)\right).

(S.67)

Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.65
), (
S.66
) and (
S.67
) we have

\displaystyle\log\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,{c})})}{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})})}=o(\log^{2}n)+\widetilde{D}_{n,1}+\log^{2}n\left(c^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{D}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}{c}_{j}P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{j}\right\|^{2}\right).

By definition of
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}=\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n,\widehat{c})}
,
c^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{D}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}{c}_{j}P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{j}\right\|^{2}
should attain its maximum value at
c=\widehat{c}
. Since the hessian matrix of this function at
c=\widehat{c}
is calculated by

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\left(c^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{D}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}{c}_{j}P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{j}\right\|^{2}\right)=\left(P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{p+1},\ldots,P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{p_{4}}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\left(P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{p+1},\ldots,P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{p_{4}}\right)\succeq 0,

this implies that the hessian matrix can only be zero matrix at
c=\widehat{c}
. Hence
P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{p+1}=\ldots=P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}}\eta_{p_{4}}=0
, i.e.,
\eta_{p+1},\ldots,\eta_{p_{4}}\in\mathcal{H}
. Hence
\widehat{\theta}_{k}=(\widehat{\theta}_{k}-\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}\widehat{c}_{j,k}\eta_{j})+\sum_{j=p+1}^{p_{4}}\widehat{c}_{j,k}\eta_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
. This implies that
\widehat{\theta}_{k}
is the unique solution of a characterization equation at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
. Hence, by excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space,
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p+1})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\ldots=(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p_{4}})^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=1
cannot happen. So
p=p_{4}
. Hence for any
j=p+1,\ldots,J
and any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds
\bar{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)}=0
for
n
large enough.

Step 3.5:
By the construction method of the (generalized) characterization equation, we have already verified the nondegeneracy condition in Proposition
8
. Then by the uniqueness result proved in Proposition
8
, we can define
\widehat{\theta}_{k}
as
\theta(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{p}^{(k)},0,\ldots,0)
. Since
(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{p}^{(k)},0,\ldots,0)\rightarrow(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p},0,\ldots,0)
, we have
\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\theta(\nu_{2}^{(k)},\ldots,\nu_{p}^{(k)},0,\ldots,0)\rightarrow\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p},0,\ldots,0)=\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
by Proposition
8
.

Step 3.6:
Finally, by (
S.64
) we have

\displaystyle 1-\nu_{j}=(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})

(S.68)

for
j=1,\ldots,p
. For
j=p+1,\ldots,J
, we have
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})\geq 1
. Since by excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space,
(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=1
cannot happen, there holds

\displaystyle(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})>1.

(S.69)

Similarly we assume that
\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}}>0
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}+1}=\ldots=\widetilde{\nu}_{J}=0
. Then for any
j=2,\ldots,\widetilde{p}
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\widetilde{\nu}_{j}=(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J}).

(S.70)

For any
j=\widetilde{p}+1,\ldots,J
, we have

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})>1.

(S.71)

Moreover,
\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
is continuous at
{(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})}
with respect to
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
and
\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
is continuous at
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
with respect to
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}})
.

Note that by the proof in Step 3, we can show that
\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J},\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J}<1
. If this is not the case, for example
\nu_{2}=1
, then we have
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\eta_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}
by (
S.68
) when
k
is large. Since
\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}\ll n
, we can easily see that
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}
while
\eta_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\theta}_{k}\leq\sum_{k=2}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{2,k}<\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}
by the construction method of characterization equation in Lemma
4
. This leads to contradiction. So we have
\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J},\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J}<1
.

Step 4:
Separate the order of summation on both sides of (
S.2.2
).

We first separate the order on the left hand side of (
S.2.2
). Denote
\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
and rank
\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bm{\nu}),j=p+1,\ldots,J
in decreasing order. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, there exists no ties among
\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bm{\nu}),\ldots,

\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{J}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bm{\nu})
. Then by (
S.69
) we can assume WLOG that
\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{p+1}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bm{\nu})>\ldots>\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{J}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bm{\nu})\geq 1+\delta
, where
\delta>0
is a positive constant.

For any given
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{p+1},\ldots,\xi_{J})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-p}
and
\bm{\bar{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p})\in(0,1)^{p-1}
, denote
T(\bm{\xi}|\bm{\bar{\nu}})=\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}[-\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)+1]
. Then we can rank all terms in
\{T(\bm{\xi}|\bm{\nu}):\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-p}\}
in decreasing order. Denote
\bm{\xi}^{(r)}
be the array such that the rank of
T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)}|\bm{\nu})
is
r
for any
r\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose the rank of
T(0,\ldots,0,1,1|\bm{\nu})
is
r^{\ast}
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, no tie exists among
T(\bm{\xi}^{(1)}|\bm{\nu}),\ldots,T(\bm{\xi}^{(r^{\ast}+1)}|\bm{\nu})
.

Now we fix
r
such that
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
. Since

\displaystyle T(\bm{\xi}|\bm{\nu})=\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}(-\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bm{\nu})+1)\leq-\delta(\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}),

by the continuity property in Proposition
8
, there exists
r_{\text{max}}\in\mathbb{N}
(which depend on
r
) and
k
large enough such that for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
, there holds

\displaystyle\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})>\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r+1)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})

(S.72)

and

\displaystyle\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})-(J+1)\geq\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r_{\text{max}}+1)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}}).

(S.73)

We assume WLOG that
r^{\ast}<r_{\text{max}}
and fix
k
large enough. For any
r<\widetilde{r}\leq r_{\text{max}}
and any
(\log\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\log\xi_{p}^{(n)},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{E}_{n,k}
, denote
{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}=(\log\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\log\xi_{p}^{(n)},\log\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\log\xi_{p}^{(r)})
for any
r,n\in\mathbb{N}
, then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}}{\log n}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log{\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)}}{\log n}\triangleq

\displaystyle(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0).

By Proposition
8
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}{\log n}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})}{\log n}=\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0).

Hence there holds

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{det}(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)}\big|{\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})))}{\operatorname{det}(-\nabla^{2}f_{n}(\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}\big|{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})))}=\exp(o(\log n)).

(S.74)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})}{\log n}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}T(\bm{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}|\bar{\bm{\nu}}),

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r)}(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})}{\log n}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r)}(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}}).

(S.75)

Furthermore, by Stirling formula, we can prove that

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})}{\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}=\exp\Big(o(\log n)+\log n\big(\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}-\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}\big)\Big).

(S.76)

Then for any
r\leq\widetilde{r}\leq r_{\text{max}}
, by (
S.2.2
), (
S.72
), (
S.74
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.76
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})}{\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(o(\log n)+\log n\big(\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}-\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{({r})}\big)\big)\times\frac{\exp\big(f_{n}(\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)}\big|{\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)}))\big)}{\exp\big(f_{n}(\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)}\big|{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}))\big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\big(\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}(\log n-\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{j}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\sum_{j=p+1}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r)}(\log n-\left(\eta_{1}-\eta_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(\widetilde{r},n)})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{j}))\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(-\log n\big[\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})-\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}T(\bm{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})+o(1)\big]\big)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0.

(S.77)

Now we control the terms with rank lower than
r_{\text{max}}
. For any
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
, denote

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)}=\underset{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n},(\xi_{p+1},\ldots,\xi_{J})\notin\{(\xi_{p+1}^{(l)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(l)}):l=1,\ldots,r_{\text{max}}\}}{\operatorname{argmax}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}).

By the same method as in Step 4 of Case 1, we can prove that, for
k
large enough and any
j=p+1,\ldots,J
, there holds

\displaystyle(\widehat{\xi}_{p+1}^{(k,n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{j}^{(k,n)}-1,\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)})\in\{(\xi_{p+1}^{(l)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(l)}):l=1,\ldots,r_{\text{max}}\}.

This implies that by fixing
k
large enough, there holds
(\widehat{\xi}_{p+1}^{(k,n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(k,n)})=(\xi_{p+1}^{(r_{\text{max}}+1)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r_{\text{max}}+1)})
when
n
is large.
Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
), (
S.73
), (
S.74
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.76
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(-\log n\big[\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\min}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})-\underset{\bar{\bm{\nu}}=(\bar{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\bar{\nu}_{p},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}{\max}T(\bm{\xi}^{(r_{\text{max}}+1)}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})+o(1)\big]\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(-\log n(J+1+o(1))\big).

(S.78)

So for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
and
k
large enough, by (
S.2.2
) and (
S.2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\sum}_{l\geq r+1}{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})}{{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\sum}_{l=r+1,\ldots,r_{\text{max}}}{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})}{{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}+\frac{{\sum}_{l\geq r_{\text{max}+1}}{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})}{{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o(1)+n^{J}\frac{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(k,n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o(1)+n^{J}\exp(-\log n(J+1+o(1)))

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0.

(S.79)

For the other side of (
S.28
), by (
S.71
) we can assume WLOG that
\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{\widetilde{p}+1}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\bm{\nu}})>\ldots>\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{1}-\widetilde{\eta}_{J}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\bm{\nu}})>1
. can similarly denote
\{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r)}:r\in\mathbb{N}\}
and
\widetilde{T}(\bm{\xi}|\bar{\bm{\nu}})
. We assume WLOG that the rank of
\widetilde{T}(0,\ldots,0,1,1|\bar{\bm{\nu}})
is higher than
r^{\ast}
. Similarly, we can prove that for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
and
k
large enough we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\sum}_{l\geq r+1}{\sum}_{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})}{{\sum}_{\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(r,n)})}\rightarrow 0.

(S.80)

Step 5:
Prove that
p=\widetilde{p}
and
\nu_{j}=\widetilde{\nu}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,p
.

Since
(\xi_{p+1}^{(1)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(1)})=\mathbf{0}
and
(\widetilde{\xi}_{\widetilde{p}+1}^{(1)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(1)})=\mathbf{0}
, by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.80
), for
k
large enough we have

\displaystyle\sum_{(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)},0,\ldots,0)\phi_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)},0,\ldots,0)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\sum_{(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{\widetilde{p}}^{(n)},0,\ldots,0)\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{\widetilde{p}}^{(n)},0,\ldots,0).

(S.81)

For notation simplicity, we only show the first
p
and
\widetilde{p}
subscripts on both sides. By similar method as in the proof in Case 1, we can show that

\displaystyle\sum_{(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)})=

\displaystyle\exp\left(n\log n\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}+n\left(\mu_{1}-\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}+o(1)\right)\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)})=

\displaystyle\exp\left(n\log n\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,k}+n\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,k}+o(1)\right)\right).

Then by (
S.2.2
) we have

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J}),

\displaystyle\mu_{1}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{1}.

Now that the result is only proved under a permutation among index
\{1,\ldots,J\}
. Now we specify the permutation and suppose there exists permutation
\pi:\{1,\ldots,J\}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,J\}
such that
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(1)}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
.

For
m=1,\ldots,J
, by Corollary
1
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{m}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{m}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)}).

We can show that
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
and
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
remain the concentration points for both sides. By similar method as in the proof of Step 6 in Case 1, for any
m=1,\ldots,J
we can derive that
\eta_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=\widetilde{\eta}_{m}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
. By the construction method of characterization equation, we can see that

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})>

\displaystyle\max_{j\neq 1}\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi(1)}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})>

\displaystyle\max_{j\neq\pi(1)}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J}).

Hence we prove that
\pi(1)=1
.
By (
S.68
) and (
S.70
), for
j=2,\ldots,p
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\nu_{j}=\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})-\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})-\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})=1-\widetilde{\nu}_{\pi^{-1}(j)}.

The last equation holds since if
\widetilde{\nu}_{\pi^{-1}(j)}=0
, then
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})-\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\theta}(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
should be strictly larger than 1 by (
S.71
).
If
\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p}
are distinct, then we can easily see that
\pi^{-1}(j)=j
for
j=1,\ldots,p
and
p=\widetilde{p}
since we assumed that
1>\nu_{2}>\ldots>\nu_{p}>0
and
1>\widetilde{\nu}_{2}>\ldots>\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}}>0
. Then the result is proved.

If
\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p}
are not distinct, for example
\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}>\ldots>\nu_{p}
, then we can prove that
\pi(2)=3,\pi(3)=2
or
\pi(2)=2,\pi(3)=3
. Hence we can still show that
\nu_{2}=\widetilde{\nu}_{2}=\nu_{3}=\widetilde{\nu}_{3}
.

Step 6:
Prove that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,p
through the dominant term in the summation.

By definition of
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
, we can easily show that
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
, where
\mathcal{G}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
are the hypercubes in layer
k
where concentration points
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
belong to, respectively. Hence
\mathcal{E}_{n,k}=\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{n,k}
for any
n,k
.

Case 1:
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
are distinct and
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}})
are distinct. Moreover, the two characterization equations at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
and
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}})
contain only type-1 equations.

Similar to Step 5 in Case 1, we can match the terms in both side in decreasing order. Then we can prove that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,p
.

Case 2:
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
are distinct and
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
are distinct. At least one of the two characterization equations at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
contain type-2 equations.

We assume WLOG that the characterization equation at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
contain type-2 equations. Furthermore, we suppose that the term in the type-2 equation is of order
\nu
. Similar to the proof in case 1, we can match the terms in both side in decreasing order. Then we can see that the characterization equation at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
should also contain type-2 equation with order
\nu
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, this indicates that the two characterization equations should be exactly the same, which implies that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,p
and
k=1,\ldots,W
.

Case 3:
There exists tie among
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
. Moreover, the two characterization equations at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
contain only type-1 equations.

For simplicity, consider the case where
p=3
and
1>\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}>0
. If the expansion of
\eta_{1}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
in both characterization equations are nondegenerated, then the two characterization equations are determined independently of
\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}
. By excluding a zero measure set in parameter set,
\eta_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=\eta_{3}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
cannot happen. This implies that
\eta_{1}
has degenerated expansion in the characterization equation, which indicates that
\eta_{1}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
. Since
\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}
, by (
S.68
) and the construction method of characterization equation,
\eta_{2}
and
\eta_{3}
should also have degenerated expansions in the characterization equation, which indicates that
\eta_{2}=\widetilde{\eta}_{2}
and
\eta_{3}=\widetilde{\eta}_{3}
. Now we should prove that
\mu_{2}=\widetilde{\mu}_{2}
and
\mu_{3}=\widetilde{\mu}_{3}
. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, suppose

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\underset{\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}:\xi_{4}=\ldots=\xi_{J}=0}{\operatorname{argmax}}~\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}){\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\underset{\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}:\xi_{4}=\ldots=\xi_{J}=0}{\operatorname{argmax}}~\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}).

By the definition of
\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\leq\sum_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\leq

\displaystyle n^{p}\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}),

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\leq\sum_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\leq

\displaystyle n^{p}\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}).

(S.82)

Then by (
S.2.2
) and (
S.2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\right|\lesssim\log n.

(S.83)

Similar to Step 5 in Case 1, we approximate
\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})
and
\log\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})
by Stirling formula and Proposition
3
and expand them in a infinite series in decreasing order. Denote the unique maximizers of
f_{n}(\theta|\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})
and
\widetilde{f}_{n}(\theta|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})
by
\widehat{\theta}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{n}
. Suppose the expansion of
\eta_{1}
,
\eta_{2}
and
\eta_{3}
in the characterization equation is as
\eta_{1}=\sum_{k}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}\alpha_{1,k}
,
\eta_{2}=\sum_{k}^{m_{2}}\gamma_{2,k}\alpha_{2,k}
and
\eta_{3}=\sum_{k}^{m_{3}}\gamma_{1,k}\alpha_{3,k}
. Define

\begin{array}[]{ll}c_{1}=\sum_{k}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}&d_{1}=\sum_{k}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}\log\frac{\gamma_{1,k}}{\omega_{1,k}},\\
c_{2}=\sum_{k}^{m_{2}}\gamma_{2,k}&d_{2}=\sum_{k}^{m_{2}}\gamma_{2,k}\log\frac{\gamma_{2,k}}{\omega_{2,k}},\\
c_{3}=\sum_{k}^{m_{3}}\gamma_{3,k}&d_{1}=\sum_{k}^{m_{3}}\gamma_{3,k}\log\frac{\gamma_{3,k}}{\omega_{3,k}}.\end{array}

Similar to Step 5 in Case 1, we have the following approximation:

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})=

\displaystyle c_{1}n\log n+(c_{1}-1)n+{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(c_{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle+{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{3}-1)\log{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(c_{3}-1)\right]+o(n^{\delta}),

where
\delta>0
is an arbitrary small constant. Moreover, we can easily show that

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+1,\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+1,\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-c_{1})\log n-(1-c_{2})\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+o(n^{-\delta}),

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-1,\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+1,\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1-c_{1})\log n+(1-c_{2})\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+(d_{1}-d_{2})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+o(n^{-\delta}),

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-c_{1})\log n-(1-c_{3})\log\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})+o(n^{-\delta}),

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}-1,\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}-1,\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1-c_{1})\log n+(1-c_{3})\log\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+(d_{1}-d_{3})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})+o(n^{-\delta}).

By the definition of
\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}
, we can derive first-order type argument as

\displaystyle\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-c_{1})\log n-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}{1-c_{2}}+o(n^{-\delta}),

\displaystyle\log\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-c_{1})\log n-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})}{1-c_{2}}+o(n^{-\delta}).

Similarly we can prove that

\displaystyle\log\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-c_{1})\log n-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\widetilde{\mu}_{2})}{1-c_{2}}+o(n^{-\delta}),

\displaystyle\log\widetilde{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-c_{1})\log n-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(\mu_{1}-\widetilde{\mu}_{3})}{1-c_{2}}+o(n^{-\delta}).

By the construction method of characterization equation we have

\displaystyle 1-\nu_{2}=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})-\eta_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=c_{1}-c_{2}\nu_{2},

\displaystyle 1-\nu_{3}=

\displaystyle\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})-\eta_{3}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=c_{1}-c_{3}\nu_{3}.

Hence
\nu_{2}=(1-c_{1})/(1-c_{2})
and
\nu_{3}=(1-c_{1})/(1-c_{3})
. This implies that
1>c_{1}>c_{2}=c_{3}
since
\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}<1
and
c_{1}>c_{2}\vee c_{3}
. For any
|k_{2}|\vee|k_{3}|\geq n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}
,

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2},\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+1,\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(n^{\delta})+(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2})\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2})-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(c_{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle-\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(c_{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle+(\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3})\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log(\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3})-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(c_{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle-\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(c_{2}-1)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(n^{\delta})+k_{2}\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(c_{2}-1)\right]+(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2})\left[(c_{2}-1)\log\frac{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2}}{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}}\right]

\displaystyle+k_{3}\left[-(c_{1}-1)\log n+(c_{2}-1)\log\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}-(d_{1}-d_{3})-(c_{2}-1)\right]+(\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3})\left[(c_{2}-1)\log\frac{\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3}}{\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(n^{\delta})-(1-c_{2})\left((\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2})\log\frac{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2}}{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}}-k_{2}\right)-(1-c_{2})\left((\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3})\log\frac{\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3}}{\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}}-k_{3}\right).

It is easy to show that
(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2})(\log(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2})-\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)})-k_{2}
and
(\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3})(\log(\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3})-\log\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)})-k_{3}
are monotone increasing in
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
when
k_{2}\geq 0
and
k_{3}\geq 0
, respectively, and are monotone decreasing in
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
when
k_{2}\leq 0
and
k_{3}\leq 0
, respectively. So when
|k_{2}|\vee|k_{3}|\geq n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}
(assume WLOG that
|k_{2}|\geq n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}
), we have

\displaystyle\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+k_{2},\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}+k_{3},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+1,\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})-\log\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o(n^{\delta})-(1-c_{2})\left((\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2})\log\frac{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}}-n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(n^{\delta})-(1-c_{2})\left((\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2})\frac{n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}}-n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(n^{\delta})-\frac{(1-c_{2})n^{\nu_{2}+\delta}}{\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-cn^{\delta},

(S.84)

where
c>0
is a constant. Similar to Step 3.2, we can also show that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}\lesssim\exp(-cn^{\delta}).

(S.85)

Define set
\mathcal{A}_{n}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n}=\big\{\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathcal{E}_{n,k}:|\xi_{2}-\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}|\vee|\xi_{3}-\widehat{\xi}_{3}^{(n)}|\leq n^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\big\}.

Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.85
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}+\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}+\frac{{\sum}_{l\geq 2}{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})\phi_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{n}:\xi_{4}=\ldots=\xi_{J}=0}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-cn^{\delta})+\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n)+\exp(-cn^{\delta})n^{J}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n).

(S.86)

Similarly, we can define
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
for the right hand side and prove that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n).

(S.87)

Then by (
S.2.2
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.87
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})-\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\right|\lesssim\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n).

(S.88)

For
m=2
, by Corollary
1
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})=\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)}).

(S.89)

We can also show that
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
and
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{J})
are the concentration points for both sides. By similar method, we can show that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}+\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}+\frac{{\sum}_{l\geq 2}{\sum}_{{\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}({\bm{\xi}}^{(l,n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{E}_{k,n}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{n}:\xi_{4}=\ldots=\xi_{J}=0}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\Delta_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{J}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-cn^{\delta})+\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n)+\exp(-cn^{\delta}+c^{\prime\prime}\log n)n^{J}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n).

(S.90)

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}{\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n).

(S.91)

By (
S.89
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.91
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})-\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})\right|\lesssim\exp(-c^{\prime}\log n)

(S.92)

For any
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}
, by the construction method of characterization equation,

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})=c_{2}\log\xi_{2}^{(n)}+d_{2}+o(n^{-\delta})=c_{2}\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+d_{2}+o(n^{-\delta}).

So we can prove that

\displaystyle\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})-\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\phi_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{2}\log\widehat{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+d_{2}+o(n^{-\delta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{2}\nu_{2}\log n+c_{2}\frac{-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}{1-c_{2}}+\mu_{2}+o(n^{-\delta}).

(S.93)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n+1}(\xi_{2}^{(n)}+1,\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(n)})-\log\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{2}\log\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)}+d_{2}+o(n^{-\delta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{2}\nu_{2}\log n+c_{2}\frac{-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\widetilde{\mu}_{2})}{1-c_{2}}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2}+o(n^{-\delta}).

(S.94)

Then by (
S.88
), (
S.92
), (
S.2.2
) and (
S.2.2
), we have

\displaystyle c_{2}\frac{-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}{1-c_{2}}+\mu_{2}-c_{2}\frac{-(d_{1}-d_{2})-(\mu_{1}-\widetilde{\mu}_{2})}{1-c_{2}}-\widetilde{\mu}_{2}=\frac{\mu_{2}-\widetilde{\mu}_{2}}{1-c_{2}}=0

Since
0<c_{2}<1
, this implies
\mu_{2}=\widetilde{\mu}_{2}
. Similarly we can prove that
\mu_{3}=\widetilde{\mu}_{3}
. Then we can similarly prove that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,p
and
k=1,\ldots,W
.

Case 4:
There exists tie among
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
or
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}})
. Moreover, at least one of the characterization equations at
(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p})
and
(\widetilde{\nu}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\nu}_{\widetilde{p}})
contain type-2 equations.

By similar method as in Cases 1 and 3, we can match all terms from type-1 equations in decreasing order. Then by similar method as in Case 2, we can match the whole characterization equation. Hence we can prove that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,p
and
k=1,\ldots,W
.

Step 6:
Prove that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,J
,
k=1,\ldots,W
and fix the permutation.

Since the continuity of
\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
and
\widetilde{\theta}(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
with respect to
\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{p}
is guaranteed by Proposition
8
, we can use similar induction method as in Step 5 of Case 1 to prove that for any
1\leq r\leq r^{\ast}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)},\xi_{p+1}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})\phi_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)},\xi_{p+1}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{p}^{(n)},\widetilde{\xi}_{p+1}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{p}^{(n)},\widetilde{\xi}_{p+1}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})

for any
(\xi_{2}^{(n)},\ldots,\xi_{p}^{(n)})
. Then by similar method, we can prove that there exists permutation
\pi:\{1,\ldots,J\}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,J\}
such that
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{\pi_{j}}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi_{j}}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi_{j_{1}}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi_{j_{2}}}
for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1.\ldots,J
and
k=1,\ldots,W
.

Finally, we use similar method as in Step 6 of Case 1 to prove that
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
. By excluding a zero measure set in the parameter space, we can assume that
\eta_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J}),\ldots,\\
\eta_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\theta(\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{J})
are distinct. So we can similarly show that
\pi=id
,
\mu_{j}=\widetilde{\mu}_{j}
,
\eta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\alpha_{k}
and
\eta_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\eta_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\eta}_{j_{2}}
. So for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1,\ldots,J
and any
0\leq t\leq s\leq t_{q+1}
, we proved that

\displaystyle\mu_{j}(t_{q+1})=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{j}(t_{q+1}),

\displaystyle Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)A_{j_{1}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s)

\displaystyle=Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)\widetilde{A}_{j_{1}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s).

If there exists multiple maximizers among
\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k},\ldots,\sum_{k=1}^{m_{J}}\gamma_{J,k}
or
\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{1,k},\ldots,\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}_{J}}\widetilde{\gamma}_{J,k}
. We assume WLOG that
\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k}=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{2}}\gamma_{2,k}
are all the maximizers among
\sum_{k=1}^{m_{1}}\gamma_{1,k},\ldots,\sum_{k=1}^{m_{J}}\gamma_{J,k}
. This indicates that
\eta_{1}
,
\eta_{2}
has degenerated expansion. So we have
\eta_{1}=\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
and
\eta_{2}=\widetilde{\eta}_{2}
.

We use similar method to prove the result by the following two steps:

Step 1:
Similarly to the Step 2 in Case 1, we first partition over
[0,1]^{J}
to find the concentration point under scaling
\bm{\xi}^{(n)}/n
. Then by similar method as in Step 2 of Case 2, we can prove that this concentration point should have zero components on the
3
-th to
J
-th subscripts.

Step 2:
Similar to Step 2 in Case 2, we then partition over
[0,1]^{J-2}
under scaling
\log\bm{\xi}^{(n)}/\log n
to find the concentration point. Similar arguments can be performed to characterize the concentration point. We can still construct characterization equation on the concentration point. Since in the characterization equation, the expansion of
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
contain disjoint terms, we can still prove continuity result which is similar to Proposition
8
around the concentration point. Then similar arguments as in Steps 3-6 can be performed to prove the result.

Case 3:

\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\|>0
,
\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\|=0
or
\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\eta_{1}}}\eta_{1}\|=0
,
\|P_{\mathcal{H}^{\perp}_{\widetilde{\eta}_{1}}}\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\|<0
.

We only discuss the first scenario. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define
\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)}={\operatorname{argmax}}_{\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi})
and suppose that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)})\widetilde{\eta}_{1}+\sum_{j=2}^{J}\bar{\xi}_{j}^{(n)}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}}{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\nu_{j}\widetilde{\eta}_{j},

where
0\leq\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{J}\leq 1
and
\sum_{j=1}^{J}\nu_{j}=1
. Since
\widetilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\eta}_{J}\in X
, we have
\sum_{j=1}^{J}\nu_{j}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}\in X
. So it is easy to verify that

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{H}_{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\nu_{j}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}}^{\perp}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\nu_{j}\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=0.

Then by part (2) in Proposition
4
, we have
\theta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})/n\rightarrow 0
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\xi}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{O}_{n}}\Delta_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\bm{\xi}^{(n)})\leq

\displaystyle J^{n}\phi_{n}(\bar{\bm{\xi}}^{(n)})=J^{n}\exp(o(n^{2}))=\exp(o(n^{2})).

(S.95)

On the other side, from the proof in Case 1, we have

\displaystyle{\phi}_{n}(\mathbf{0})=\exp(o(n^{2})+n^{2}\|P_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta_{1}}^{\perp}}\eta_{1}\|^{2}).

(S.96)

Then (
S.28
), (
S.95
) and (
S.96
) lead to contradiction.

So by induction method, we prove that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}
and
0\leq t\leq s\leq T
, with probability 1 there holds

\displaystyle\beta_{j_{0}}+\beta_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}X_{j}(t)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}_{j_{0}}+\widetilde{\beta}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}X_{j}(t),

\displaystyle Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)A_{j_{1}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s)

\displaystyle=Z_{j_{1}}^{\mathrm{T}}(t)\widetilde{A}_{j_{1}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}(s).

By Condition (d), this implies that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1,\ldots,J
,
\beta_{j_{0}}=\widetilde{\beta}_{j_{0}}
,
\beta_{j}=\widetilde{\beta}_{j}
and
A_{j_{1}}\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}=\widetilde{A}_{j_{1}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}_{j_{2}}
. So we have
A\Sigma A^{\mathrm{T}}=\widetilde{A}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{T}}
. By Condition (c), there exists a permutation matrix
C
and
\widetilde{C}
such that
CA=(I_{D},R^{\mathrm{T}})^{\mathrm{T}}
and
\widetilde{C}\widetilde{A}=(I_{D},\widetilde{R}^{\mathrm{T}})^{\mathrm{T}}
. Then it is easy to show that
C^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma C=\widetilde{C}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{C}
. Since
\widetilde{C}C^{\mathrm{T}}
is again a permutation matrix, there exists permutation matrix
B=\widetilde{C}C^{\mathrm{T}}
such that
B\Sigma B^{\mathrm{T}}=\widetilde{\Sigma}
. Now we have
C^{\mathrm{T}}R\Sigma C=\widetilde{C}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{R}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{C}
, which implies that
C^{\mathrm{T}}R\Sigma C=\widetilde{C}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{R}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{C}
. So we have
BR\Sigma B^{\mathrm{T}}=\widetilde{R}\widetilde{\Sigma}=\widetilde{R}B\Sigma B^{\mathrm{T}}
, which implies that
\widetilde{R}=BRB^{\mathrm{T}}
. Finally it is easy to show that
\widetilde{A}B=A
, i.e.,
(A,\Sigma)\sim(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{\Sigma})
. Hence the identifiability result is proved.
∎

S.2.3
Proof of Proposition
1
and Corollary
1

Proof of Proposition
1
.

For any
t\in[0,T]
, there exists
t_{0}>0
such that no events occur on interval
(t,t+t_{0}]
and the two intensity functions remain constant on
(t,t+t_{0}]
. Then for any
0<\Delta t<t_{0}
, by matching the likelihood function in the two competing models on
[0,t+\Delta t]
, we have

\displaystyle\int\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Big[\prod_{s\leq t}(\lambda_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)})e^{-\int_{0}^{\mathrm{t}}\lambda_{j}(s)ds}\Big]\exp\Big(-\Delta t\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(t+0)\Big)\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\Sigma)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Big[\prod_{s\leq t}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)})e^{-\int_{0}^{\mathrm{t}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)ds}\Big]\exp\Big(-\Delta t\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t+0)\Big)\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\widetilde{\Sigma})d\theta\text{.}

(S.97)

For any
n
, we take the
n
-th derivative of both sides in equation (
S.2.3
) with respect to
\Delta t
and let
\Delta t\downarrow 0
, then we have

\displaystyle\int\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{s\leq t}\lambda_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)}\Big]\exp\Big(-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{j}(s)ds\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(t+0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\Sigma)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{s\leq t}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)}\Big]\exp\Big(-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)ds\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t+0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\widetilde{\Sigma})d\theta\text{.}

Thus the proposition is proved.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1
.

For any
t\in[0,T]
, there exists
t_{0}>0
such that no events occur on interval
(t,t+t_{0}]
and the two intensity functions remain constant on
(t,t+t_{0}]
. For any
0<\Delta t<t_{0}
, we consider a hypothesized sample path on interval
[0,t+\Delta t]
which has the same trajectory on
[0,t+\Delta t)
but has the
m
-th event happening at time
t+\Delta t
. The hypothesized sample path has positive density. Since the intensity functions is adapted to the natural filtration and is left-continuous, the intensity function on the hypothesized sample path is the same as the observed sample path on
[0,t+\Delta t]
. Then by matching the likelihood functions on
[0,t+\Delta t]
in the hypothesized sample path, we have

\displaystyle\int\lambda_{m}(t+0)\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Big[\prod_{s\leq t}(\lambda_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)})e^{-\int_{0}^{\mathrm{t}}\lambda_{j}(s)ds}\Big]\exp\Big(-\Delta t\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(t+0)\Big)\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\Sigma)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\widetilde{\lambda}_{m}(t+0)\prod_{j=1}^{J}\Big[\prod_{s\leq t}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)})e^{-\int_{0}^{\mathrm{t}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)ds}\Big]\exp\Big(-\Delta t\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t+0)\Big)\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\widetilde{\Sigma})d\theta\text{.}

(S.98)

For any
n
, we take the
n
-th derivative of both sides in equation (
S.2.3
) with respect to
\Delta t
and let
\Delta t\downarrow 0
to obtain

\displaystyle\int\lambda_{m}(t+0)\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{s\leq t}\lambda_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)}\Big]\exp\Big(-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{j}(s)ds\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{j}(t+0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\Sigma)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\widetilde{\lambda}_{m}(t+0)\Big[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\prod_{s\leq t}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)^{\Delta N_{j}(s)}\Big]\exp\Big(-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)ds\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(t+0)\Big)^{n}\phi_{K}(\theta;0,\widetilde{\Sigma})d\theta\text{.}

∎

S.2.4
Proof of Proposition
2

Proof of Proposition
2
.

We only prove the case when
\left\{\widetilde{y}_{ij}:1\leq i\leq j\leq J\right\}
are also distinct. We assume WLOG that
y_{11}
is the unique largest term among
y_{11},\ldots,y_{JJ}
since they are distinct. Furthermore, by (
2
) we assume WLOG that
y_{11}>y_{12}>\ldots>y_{1J}
. Suppose that
\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{1}}
is the unique largest term among
\widetilde{y}_{11},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{JJ}
and suppose
\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{1}}>\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}>\ldots>\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{J}}
, where
\left\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{J}\right\}
is a permutation of
\left\{1,\ldots,J\right\}
. Let
\pi(1)=j_{1},\ldots,\pi(J)=j_{J}
. In the following part, we prove that for any
j,j_{1},j_{2}=1,\ldots,J
,
x_{j}=\widetilde{x}_{\pi(j)}
and
y_{j_{1}j_{2}}=\widetilde{y}_{\pi(j_{1})\pi(j_{2})}
. For notation simplicity, we assume WLOG that
\pi(1)=1,\ldots,\pi(J)=J
.

The following proof consists of two steps. In the first step, we prove that the summations on both sides of (
S.7
) can be separated in order, where each term dominates the summation of all terms with lower rank. In the second step, we prove that the dominant terms on both sides can match exactly. Then by induction method, we can match every terms on both sides.

Step 1:
For any
(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{J-1}
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, denote

\displaystyle T(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=2}^{J}\left(\frac{y_{1j}}{y_{11}}\right)^{\xi_{j}}\text{,}

\displaystyle S_{n}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}}y_{11}^{\left(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}\right)^{2}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}\left(x_{j}^{\xi_{j}}y_{1j}^{2\xi_{j}\left(n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}\right)}\right)\prod_{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{\xi_{j_{1}}\xi_{j_{2}}}.

We rank all the components in
\left\{T(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J}):~\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J}\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
in decreasing order. For any
r\in\mathbb{N}
, Denote
(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})
be the array such that the rank of
T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})
is
r
and denote
K_{r}=\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r)}
. We assume that there are no ties in the rank (If there are ties, then similar proof can be performed by putting the tie terms together). Define

\displaystyle\Delta_{r,n}=\binom{n}{n-\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}^{(r)},\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)}}.

Then we can simplify the summation on the left hand side of (
S.7
) as

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}\leq J}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{n}x_{j_{k}}\prod_{1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq n}y_{j_{k_{1}}j_{k_{2}}}\right)=\sum_{r\geq 1}\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)}).

Since
y_{11}>y_{12}>\ldots>y_{1J}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{T(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}{T(0,\ldots,0)}\leq\left(\frac{y_{12}}{y_{11}}\right)^{\sum_{j=2}^{J}\xi_{j}}\text{.}

Hence for any fixed
r
, there exists
r_{\text{max}}
such that

\displaystyle\underset{u>r_{\text{max}}}{\max}T(\xi_{2}^{(u)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(u)})\leq\frac{1}{J}T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})\text{.}

(S.99)

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{r_{\text{max}}}\Delta_{r,n}\leq n^{r_{\text{max}}}J^{r_{\text{max}}}\text{.}

(S.100)

We assume WLOG that for any
u\geq r_{\text{max}}
, there holds
K_{u}>K_{r}
. Then for any
\widetilde{r}
such that
\widetilde{r}>r
, we discuss the following two cases:

Case 1:
If
K_{\widetilde{r}}\geq K_{r}
, then

\displaystyle~~~~\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})}{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{1}^{n-K_{\widetilde{r}}}y_{11}^{(n-K_{\widetilde{r}})^{2}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}x_{j}^{\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}}y_{1j}^{2\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}(n-K_{\widetilde{r}})}\prod_{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{\xi_{j_{1}}^{(\widetilde{r})}\xi_{j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{r})}}}{x_{1}^{n-K_{r}}y_{11}^{(n-K_{r})^{2}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}x_{j}^{\xi_{j}^{(r)}}y_{1j}^{2\xi_{j}^{(r)}(n-K_{r})}\prod_{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{\xi_{j_{1}}^{(r)}\xi_{j_{2}}^{(r)}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\max}x_{j}}{\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\min}x_{j}}\right]^{K_{\widetilde{r}}}\left[\frac{y_{11}}{\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\max}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}}\right]^{-(K_{r}-K_{\widetilde{r}})^{2}}\left[\frac{\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\max}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}}{\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\min}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}}\right]^{K_{\widetilde{r}}^{2}-(K_{\widetilde{r}}-K_{r})^{2}}\left[\frac{y_{11}^{-K_{\widetilde{r}}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}y_{1j}^{\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}}}{y_{11}^{-K_{r}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}y_{1j}^{\xi_{j}^{(r)}}}\right]^{2(n-K_{\widetilde{r}})}

\displaystyle=C_{1}^{K_{\widetilde{r}}}C_{2}^{-(K_{r}-K_{\widetilde{r}})^{2}}C_{3}^{K_{\widetilde{r}}^{2}-(K_{\widetilde{r}}-K_{r})^{2}}\left[\frac{T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{T(\xi_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})}\right]^{-2(n-K_{\widetilde{r}})}\text{,}

(S.101)

where
C_{1}=\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\max}x_{j}/\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\min}x_{j}
,
C_{2}=y_{11}/\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\max}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}>1
and
C_{3}=\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\max}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}/\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\min}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}
are constants that does not depend on the choice of
n
,
r
or
\widetilde{r}
.

Furthermore, for
u>r_{\text{max}}
, by (
S.99
) and (
S.2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(u)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(u)})}{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{K_{u}}C_{2}^{-(K_{r}-K_{u})^{2}}C_{3}^{K_{u}^{2}-(K_{\widetilde{r}}-K_{r})^{2}}\left[\frac{T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{T(\xi_{2}^{(u)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(u)})}\right]^{-2(n-K_{u})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{K_{u}}C_{2}^{-(K_{r}-K_{u})^{2}}C_{3}^{K_{u}^{2}-(K_{u}-K_{r})^{2}}J^{-2(n-K_{u})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle J^{-2n}\max_{K>K_{r}}\left\{C_{1}^{K}C_{2}^{-(K_{r}-K)^{2}}C_{3}^{K^{2}-(K-K_{r})^{2}}J^{2K}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle J^{-2n}

(S.102)

since
C_{2}>1
.

Case 2:
If
K_{\widetilde{r}}<K_{r}
, then

\displaystyle~~~~\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})}{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{1}^{n-K_{\widetilde{r}}}y_{11}^{(n-K_{\widetilde{r}})^{2}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}x_{j}^{\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}}y_{1j}^{2\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}(n-K_{\widetilde{r}})}\prod_{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{\xi_{j_{1}}^{(\widetilde{r})}\xi_{j_{1}}^{(\widetilde{r})}}}{x_{1}^{n-K_{r}}y_{11}^{(n-K_{r})^{2}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}x_{j}^{\xi_{j}^{(r)}}y_{1j}^{2\xi_{j}^{(r)}(n-K_{r})}\prod_{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}^{\xi_{j_{1}}^{(r)}\xi_{j_{1}}^{(r)}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\max}x_{j}}{\underset{j=1,\ldots,J}{\min}x_{j}}\right]^{K_{r}}\left[\frac{\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\max}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}}{\underset{2\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq J}{\min}y_{j_{1}j_{2}}}\right]^{K_{r}^{2}}\left[\frac{y_{11}^{-K_{\widetilde{r}}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}y_{1j}^{\xi_{j}^{(\widetilde{r})}}}{y_{11}^{-K_{r}}\prod_{j=2}^{J}y_{1j}^{\xi_{j}^{(r)}}}\right]^{2(n-K_{r})}

\displaystyle=C_{1}^{K_{\widetilde{r}}}C_{3}^{K_{r}^{2}}\left[\frac{T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{T(\xi_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})}\right]^{-2(n-K_{r})}\text{.}

(S.103)

By (
S.100
), (
S.2.4
) and (
S.2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{u\geq r+1}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{u=r+1,\ldots,r_{\text{max}}}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}+\frac{\sum_{u\geq r_{\text{max}}+1}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle n^{r_{\text{max}}}J^{r_{\text{max}}}\left(\frac{T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{T(\xi_{2}^{(r+1)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r+1)})}\right)^{-2n}+J^{n}J^{-2n}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0\text{.}

(S.104)

Similarly we define
\widetilde{T}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
,
\widetilde{S}_{n}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
,
(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}
for the right hand side of (
S.7
).
Then we can prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum_{u>r}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}=0.

(S.105)

This finishes the proof in step 1.

Step 2:
Under the introduced notation, equation (
S.7
) turns into

\displaystyle\sum_{u\geq 1}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})=\sum_{u\geq 1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})

(S.106)

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We then use induction method to prove that for any
r,n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})\text{,}

\displaystyle T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{T}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})\text{,}

\displaystyle S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})\text{.}

When
r=1
, by assumption it is easy to see that
(\xi^{(1)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(1)}_{J})=(\widetilde{\xi}^{(1)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(1)}_{J})=(0,\ldots,0)
. By (
S.2.4
) and (
S.105
) we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum_{u\geq 1}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{\Delta_{1,n}S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}=1\text{.}

(S.107)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum_{u\geq 1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{1,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}=1\text{.}

(S.108)

By (
S.106
), (
S.107
) and (
S.108
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\Delta_{1,n}S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{1,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\Delta_{1,n}S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}{\sum_{u\geq 1}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}\frac{\sum_{u\geq 1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{1,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\text{.}

(S.109)

By the definition of
S_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}=\frac{x_{1}^{n}y_{11}^{n^{2}}}{\widetilde{x}_{1}^{n}\widetilde{y}_{11}^{n^{2}}}.

Then (
S.2.4
) implies that
x_{1}=\widetilde{x}_{1}
,
y_{11}=\widetilde{y}_{11}
,
S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)=\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)
and
T(0,\ldots,0)=\widetilde{T}(0,\ldots,0)
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence the result is proved for
r=1
.

Suppose that the result is proved for
1,\ldots,r-1
. By induction assumption and (
S.106
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{u\geq r}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\geq 1}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})-\sum_{1\leq u<r}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\geq 1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})-\sum_{1\leq u<r}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\geq r}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})\text{.}

(S.110)

For
j=1,\ldots,J
, define
\tau_{j}=\min\{r\in\mathbb{N}:\xi_{j}^{(r)}>0\}
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{j}=\min\{r\in\mathbb{N}:\widetilde{\xi}_{j}^{(r)}>0\}
. Since
y_{11}>\ldots>y_{1J}
, it is easy to see that
\tau_{1}<\ldots<\tau_{J}
. Suppose that
\tau_{l}<r\leq\tau_{l+1}
(define
\tau_{0}=0
and
\tau_{J+1}=\infty
). By induction assumption, we also have
\widetilde{\tau}_{l}<r\leq\widetilde{\tau}_{l+1}
. There exists
\widetilde{r}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})=(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})
. We then prove that
r=\widetilde{r}
. The proof falls into four cases:

Case 1:
r<\tau_{l+1}
and
\widetilde{r}<r
. By induction assumption, we have
(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})=(\xi_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})
, which implies that
(\xi_{2}^{(\widetilde{r})},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})})=(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})
. This leads to contradiction.

Case 2:
r<\tau_{l+1}
and
\widetilde{r}>r
. For any
j=1,\ldots,l
, let
\xi_{j}=1
and
\xi_{2}=\ldots=\xi_{j-1}=\xi_{j+1}=\ldots=\xi_{J}=0
. It is easy to show that the rank of
(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
is exactly
\tau_{j}
, which is smaller than
r
. So by induction assumption, we have

\displaystyle 1=\frac{T(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}{\widetilde{T}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}=\frac{y_{1j}}{\widetilde{y}_{1j}},

which implies that
y_{1j}=\widetilde{y}_{1j}
for
j=1,\ldots,l
. Then by (
S.2.4
), (
S.2.4
) and induction assumption, there holds

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{J})}=

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}{S_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}\frac{\widetilde{S}_{n}(0,\ldots,0)}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{J})}\asymp\left[\frac{T(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}{\widetilde{T}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{J})}\right]^{2n}=1

(S.111)

since
\xi_{l+1}^{(r)}=\ldots=\xi_{J}^{(r)}=\widetilde{\xi}_{l+1}^{(\widetilde{r})}=\ldots=\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(\widetilde{r})}=0
. However, by (
S.2.4
), (
S.105
) and (
S.2.4
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{J})}{S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{\widetilde{r},n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{J})}{\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{\widetilde{r},n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(\widetilde{r})}_{J})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}{\sum_{u\geq r}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})}\frac{\sum_{u\geq r}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{u\geq r+1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}{\sum_{u\geq r}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})}\frac{\sum_{u\geq r}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(u)}_{J})}{\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0,

which contradicts with (
S.111
).

Case 3:
r<\tau_{l+1}
and
\widetilde{r}=r
. The result is proved.

Case 4:
r=\tau_{l+1}
and
r<\widetilde{r}_{l+1}
. By similar method as in Case 1 and Case 2, this leads to contradiction.

Case 5:
r=\tau_{l+1}
and
r=\widetilde{\tau}_{l+1}
. Then it is easy to prove that
(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})=(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})=(0,\ldots,0,1,0,\ldots,0)
where all components are 0 except that the
l
-th component is 1. This implies that
r=\widetilde{r}
.

Now we have proved that
r=\widetilde{r}
, i.e.,
(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})=(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})
. This indicates that
\Delta_{r,n}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}
. Then by (
S.2.4
), (
S.105
) and (
S.2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\Delta_{r,n}S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{\sum_{u\geq r}\Delta_{u,n}S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(u)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(u)})}\frac{\sum_{u\geq r}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(u)}_{J})}{\widetilde{\Delta}_{r,n}\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1.

(S.112)

By the definition of
S_{n}
, there exists constant
D_{1},D_{2},D_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}=D_{1}D_{2}^{n}D_{3}^{n^{2}}.

Then (
S.2.4
) indicates that
D_{1}=D_{2}=D_{3}=1
, i.e.,
S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})=\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})
for any
n
. Similarly to (
S.111
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}\asymp\left[\frac{T(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})}{\widetilde{T}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})}\right]^{2n}.

Then we have
T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})=\widetilde{T}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})
, which finishes the proof for case
r
.

Hence by induction method, we proved that for any
r,n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle(\xi^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\xi^{(r)}_{J})

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})\text{,}

\displaystyle T(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{T}(\widetilde{\xi}_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}_{J}^{(r)})\text{,}

\displaystyle S_{n}(\xi_{2}^{(r)},\ldots,\xi_{J}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{S}_{n}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{\xi}^{(r)}_{J})\text{.}

For any
j=2,\ldots,J
, let
(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
be the array such that

\displaystyle\xi_{m}=\begin{cases}1~~~&m=j\\
0~~~&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}=\frac{x_{j}y_{1j}^{2(n-1)}y_{jj}}{\widetilde{x}_{j}\widetilde{y}_{1j}^{2(n-1)}\widetilde{y}_{jj}}\text{,}

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{n}(2\xi_{2},\ldots,2\xi_{J})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(2\xi_{2},\ldots,2\xi_{J})}=\frac{x_{j}^{2}y_{1j}^{4(n-2)}y^{4}_{jj}}{\widetilde{x}_{j}^{2}\widetilde{y}_{1j}^{4(n-2)}\widetilde{y}^{4}_{jj}}\text{.}

This implies that
x_{j}=\widetilde{x}_{j}
,
y_{1j}=\widetilde{y}_{1j}
and
y_{jj}=\widetilde{y}_{jj}
. For any
2\leq j_{1}<j_{2}\leq J
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})
be

\displaystyle\xi_{m}=\begin{cases}1~~~&m=j_{1}\text{ or }j_{2}\\
0~~~&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

Then we have

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{n}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}{\widetilde{S}_{n}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{J})}=\frac{y_{j_{1}j_{2}}}{\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}}\text{.}

This implies that
y_{j_{1}j_{2}}=\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}j_{2}}
. Hence the proposition is proved.
∎

S.2.5
Proof of Proposition
3

To prove Proposition
3
, we first verify the following lemma:

Lemma 1
.

Let
f(x)
be a strictly concave functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
0
as its unique maximizer. Assume that
-\nabla^{2}f(x)\succeq I_{d}
holds at any point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then for any
\delta>0
, there holds

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{x:\left\|x\right\|\geq C}\exp(f(x))dx}{\int_{x}\exp(f(x))dx}\leq\delta,

where
C>0
is a constant that is independent of
f
.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

We change variable to
d
-dimensional polar coordinates:

\displaystyle\int_{x:\left\|x\right\|\geq C}\exp(f(x))dx=\int_{\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1}}\left(\prod_{k=2}^{d-1}\sin^{k-1}\theta_{k}\right)d\theta_{1}\ldots,d\theta_{d-1}\int_{r\geq C}r^{d-1}\exp(f(r\alpha(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1})))dr,

\displaystyle\int_{x:\left\|x\right\|\leq C}\exp(f(x))dx=\int_{\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1}}\left(\prod_{k=2}^{d-1}\sin^{k-1}\theta_{k}\right)d\theta_{1}\ldots,d\theta_{d-1}\int_{r\leq C}r^{d-1}\exp(f(r\alpha(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1})))dr,

(S.113)

where
\left\|\alpha(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1})\right\|=1
. For fixed
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
C>0
, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{d}{dr}\Bigg|_{r=C}f(r\alpha)=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\nabla f(r\alpha)\Big|_{r=C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\left(\nabla f(0)+r\nabla^{2}f(x_{r}^{\ast})\alpha\right)\Big|_{r=C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\left(-\nabla^{2}f(x_{r}^{\ast})\right)\alpha\Big|_{r=C}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle r\left\|\alpha\right\|^{2}\Big|_{r=C}=C

(S.114)

since
-\nabla^{2}f(x_{r}^{\ast})\succeq I_{d}
. Similarly we have

\displaystyle-\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}\Bigg|_{r=C}f(r\alpha)=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{\mathrm{T}}\nabla^{2}f(r\alpha)\Big|_{r=C}\alpha\geq\left\|\alpha\right\|^{2}=1.

(S.115)

We choose
C
large enough such that for any
r\geq C
, there holds:
r^{d-1}\exp\left(-Cr-\frac{1}{2}r^{2}\right)\leq(r+\frac{1}{2}C)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}Cr-\frac{1}{2}r^{2}\right)
.
Then by (
1
) and (
S.115
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{r\geq C}r^{d-1}\exp(f(r\alpha(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1})))dr\leq

\displaystyle\exp(f(C\alpha))\int_{r\geq C}r^{d-1}\exp(-Cr-\frac{1}{2}r^{2})dr

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(f(C\alpha))\int_{r\geq C}\big(r+\frac{1}{2}C\big)\exp(-\frac{1}{2}Cr-\frac{1}{2}r^{2})dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(f(C\alpha)-C^{2}).

(S.116)

On the other hand, by similar arguments as in (
1
) we can see that
\exp(f(r\alpha))
is monotonely decreasing for
r\geq 0
. So we have

\displaystyle\int_{r\leq C}r^{d-1}\exp(f(r\alpha(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1})))dr\geq\exp(f(C\alpha))\int_{r\leq C}r^{d-1}dr=\frac{C^{d}\exp(f(C\alpha))}{d}.

(S.117)

By (
1
), (
1
) and (
S.117
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{x:\left\|x\right\|\geq C}\exp(f(x))dx}{\int_{x:\left\|x\right\|\leq C}\exp(f(x))dx}\leq\frac{d}{C^{d}}\exp(-C^{2}).
